25:Tb4e,Fixes #17521.

## What changed

- runs every `Quality (Fork)` lane on `ubuntu-24.04`, removing persistent workspace and runner-service state from untrusted fork validation
- pins the fork lane to the canonical released CI Bun version (`1.3.14`)
- isolates setup-bun's Unix `HOME` and Windows `USERPROFILE` by run, attempt, job, and matrix entry in the shared workspace action, cloud setup action, and direct CLI lane
- disables setup-bun's executable cache for those ephemeral paths, preventing per-run cache-version churn while preserving package and Turbo caches
- separates manual exact-head dispatch concurrency from pull-request metadata runs so skipped PR jobs cannot cancel queued proof
- enforces hosted selection for every current and future fork job, derives the Bun pin from `.github/ci-bun-version.json`, binds isolated homes to parsed setup-bun steps, and rejects same-repository pull requests through mutation tests

## Why

Quality (Fork) run 30688698836 assigned Build and CLI smoke to two registrations on the same Hetzner host. Both were canceled inside actions/checkout while `git clean -ffdx` was running; no setup or repository command executed. GitHub's recorded job completion timestamps precede the checkout logs, and the same registrations have produced prior canceled jobs after successful commands. Hosted runners provide a fresh workspace and process boundary for this security-sensitive fork lane.

Failure evidence:
- Build: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30688698836/job/91339469729
- CLI smoke: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30688698836/job/91339469735

<!-- evidence-head:e2d936f6052173da04d5d59419316251ac53e729 -->27:T1883,Closes #17602

`providerExecutionsInFlight` stored one promise per `message.id + provider + audience`, wired to the **first** caller's signal and deadline; a later coalesced caller awaited it verbatim (`runtime.ts:4645`, raw `await execution.promise`). Because the turn registry maps a room to its **newest** controller (`turn-controller.ts` `runWith` overwrites without aborting), the realistic sequence is:

1. Turn A is streaming; its `composeState` owns the execution.
2. Turn B (re-delivery of the same message) starts, coalesces onto A's execution.
3. The user taps **stop** → `abortTurn(room)` aborts **B's** controller.
4. Nothing happens. B neither rejects nor resolves until A's provider settles, then **completes as if never cancelled** — the stop is swallowed. Symmetrically, an abort on A's side rejected B with **A's** `TurnAbortedError`.

## The fix

The execution now owns a dedicated `AbortController`, and every caller — owner included — awaits through `awaitProviderExecution`: a per-waiter race against the caller's **own** signal, reference-counted:

- an aborting waiter rejects immediately with **its** reason;
- the shared work keeps running for the remaining callers;
- the provider is aborted exactly when the last interested caller is gone, so a lone caller's abort still reaches the provider (the existing cancellation test's assertions — `PROVIDER_COMPOSITION_ABORTED` report, `TURN_ABORTED` surface, reason propagation — are unchanged and still green).

No behavior change for uncoalesced executions beyond routing the same signal through the same boundary; deadlines (`withProviderDeadline`) are untouched and still shared per-execution as documented.

## Proof

<img width="1529" height="612" alt="17604-red-green" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/da6d194a-ad80-49a4-96e0-cb844010a6e8" />

Deterministic regression test (`compose-state-provider-execution.test.ts`, "lets a coalesced waiter cancel its own turn without killing the owner"): two overlapping `runWith` turns on one room, one shared SLOW provider, `abortTurn` after coalescing, 250ms race to catch the swallow.

<details><summary>red on the previous code → green with the fix (verbatim)</summary>

```
$ bun run --cwd packages/core test -- compose-state-provider-execution   # test added, code unfixed
 Tests  1 failed | 5 passed (6)
 AssertionError: expected 'swallowed' to be an instance of TurnAbortedError
 (the coalesced waiter neither rejected nor resolved 250ms after abortTurn — the user's stop was swallowed)

$ bun run --cwd packages/core test -- compose-state                      # after fix, all compose-state suites
 Test Files  8 passed (8)
 Tests  32 passed (32)

$ bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck
 Done!

$ bunx biome check packages/core/src/runtime.ts packages/core/src/__tests__/compose-state-provider-execution.test.ts
 Checked 2 files. No fixes applied.
```
</details>

## Relation to #17508

#17508 replaces the deadline architecture with owner cancellation but keeps the same coalescing shape, so this defect survives there (re-derived in https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/17508#issuecomment-5150514957; @lalalune independently reproduced it on the prior head). This fix is deliberately small and boundary-shaped: if #17508 lands first I will rebase this onto its `withProviderCancellation` form — the new test asserts waiter-boundary *semantics*, not implementation, so it transfers as-is.

# Risks

Medium. Config persistence is startup-critical. The fallback is limited to Linux-style `EBUSY` replacement failures, writes a complete sanitized overlay using an exclusive randomized 0600 temp file + file fsync + rename + directory fsync, and throws if that commit also fails. Existing overlays remain the write target for canonical config saves so stale state cannot override a later base write. An explicit `ELIZA_PERSIST_CONFIG_PATH` disables the automatic overlay entirely and remains authoritative.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes two failures exposed while enabling first-party views in a container:

1. `saveElizaConfig()` always replaced `eliza.json` with `rename(2)`. A single-file Docker bind mount cannot be replaced and returns `EBUSY`, so plugin enablement appeared live but disappeared after restart. On exactly that error, the agent now atomically commits the full sanitized config to `<state-dir>/<namespace>.config-overlay.json`; `loadElizaConfig()` merges it last on canonical-config boots. An explicit persistence path bypasses the automatic overlay so stale keys cannot leak across deployment modes. Any fallback failure is surfaced, never ignored.
2. Initial startup applies `ELIZA_AGENT_CHARACTER_JSON` and pins `SANDBOX_ROUTE_AGENT_ID`, but the hot-reload closure rebuilt directly from disk config. A plugin-triggered restart could therefore replace the injected Sol character with default Eliza and a name-derived UUID. Initial boot and hot reload now share `applySandboxIdentityFromEnv`, and reload re-pins the routing id.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix, non-breaking.

# Testing

## Develop-red proof

- Bind-mount regression against the develop implementation throws `EBUSY` at `renameSync(tmp, /cfg/eliza.json)` and creates no durable overlay.
- Develop's hot-reload path calls `loadElizaConfig()` followed directly by `buildCharacterFromConfig(freshConfig)`; it never reapplies `ELIZA_AGENT_CHARACTER_JSON` or `SANDBOX_ROUTE_AGENT_ID`.

## Head-green proof

- `config-bind-mount-persistence.test.ts`: simulates canonical rename `EBUSY`, verifies a 0600 durable overlay, unchanged base mount, restart load, second write, fail-closed dual-write failure, explicit-persist precedence over stale overlays, and include/wallet-key sanitization.
- `sandbox-character.test.ts`: applies the same injected Sol identity to initial and fresh reload configs and verifies the stable route id.
- Focused result: **2 files, 18 tests passed**.
- Biome on all five changed files: clean.
- `git diff --check`: clean.
- Package typecheck reaches only unrelated missing optional workspace declarations in the isolated worktree; the prior exact-head CI typecheck passed and the new exact-head CI run is authoritative.

2b:T13f8,## Summary

Discord can emit `messageCreate` immediately after gateway `ClientReady` while async `onReady` is still hydrating Discord application-owner aliases. The account facade also snapshotted `clientReadyPromise` while it was still `null`, because `MessageManager` is constructed before `initializeAccount()` assigns the promise.

An owner message in that window was persisted under the platform-derived entity instead of the canonical owner. Owner-private recall then correctly failed closed for the wrong identity, leaving split identity in canonical memory.

This PR:

- exposes the account ready promise through a live facade getter;
- gates gateway ingress, including listen-only ingestion, until readiness;
- awaits readiness again in MessageManager before Discord dedupe, entity/room persistence, audience attestation, model context, or delivery;
- bounds the readiness wait at 30 seconds and fails closed with `discord:message-before-ready` when ready-time initialization rejects or stalls;
- adds real AgentRuntime + plugin-sql/PGlite production-path proof through `MessageManager`, canonical owner/room participants, destination-bound recall authorization, and exactly one Discord DM send.

## Block-0 and overlap

Checked open PRs and elizaOS Project 12 before code. No open item owns this race.

- #17212 is merged and owns destination-bound private-context policy. This PR uses it unchanged. Owner-in-group stays DENY.
- #17213 is merged and owns optional group coordination. This PR does not change it or its default-off posture.
- #17167 is closed; its outbound DM participant-before-dedupe fix is already on `develop`. This PR does not duplicate outbound delivery scope.
- Canonical-memory and deployment lanes retain their schema/deploy ownership. This PR only closes pre-ingress identity ordering.

## Production path

`service.ts initializeAccount/attemptDiscordLogin` -> `discord-interactions.ts onReady/refreshOwnerDiscordUserIds` -> `discord-events.ts messageCreate/ready gate` -> `messages.ts handleMessage/defense gate` -> `discord-history.ts buildMemoryFromMessage` -> core `ensureConnection` -> canonical delivery-audience attestation -> core MessageService persistence/composition -> Discord callback/send/persist.

## Bidirectional proof

`connector-loop.harness.test.ts` boots a real PGlite-backed `AgentRuntime` with deterministic model proxy and drives the production connector entrypoint.

With the production fix reverted, it fails because one DM is sent before owner hydration (`expected 0, received 1`). With this head it proves:

- no room, memory, or send before readiness;
- one external DM after readiness;
- persisted actor is the canonical owner;
- room participants are exactly owner + runtime agent;
- stored Discord text is recallable;
- owner-private authorization succeeds in DM;
- adding a third participant makes authorization fail closed.

A separate facade regression fails on unmodified develop because it receives the stale snapshotted promise, and passes on head.

2d:T12a7,# Relates to

Closes #17588.

# Risk

- [x] This PR targets `develop` and is rebased onto `origin/develop@9bdcf068bd88d39c82927a861df810d0c7810da1` with zero conflicts.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the parser behavior and its failure mode from the executable tests and mutation record below.

# Background

`check-pr-evidence.mjs` accepts pasted transcripts for non-visual evidence rows, but its help advertised only links and `N/A`. More importantly, `boundRowBlock` stopped at a blank line inside a fenced transcript. The most natural Markdown form therefore looked valid to an author while the gate silently saw only the checkbox line.

# Implementation

- The failure help now documents all supported evidence forms and the actual `3` line / `120` character transcript floor.
- Transcript measurement and shortfall diagnostics share one parsing path, so help cannot drift from acceptance.
- Evidence rows now preserve complete backtick and tilde fences across a blank line before the fence and blank lines inside it.
- Fence closing follows Markdown semantics: the delimiter character must match and the closer must be at least as long as the opener.
- Unterminated fences fail closed and are truncated before the opener, preventing unrelated headings or links below a row from becoming accidental evidence.
- Regression cases cover complete backtick fences, tilde fences, blank lines, threshold diagnostics, and unterminated-fence bleed.

This is the implementation-level repair. The earlier draft merely documented the fenced-block limitation; that would have left a standard Markdown transcript broken.

# Verification

Exact repaired head: `cbbe424a217a489ed50a3af431efe586d31fab47`
<!-- evidence-head:cbbe424a217a489ed50a3af431efe586d31fab47 -->

- `node scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs --self-test`: 14/14 cases passed.
- `node --test scripts/check-pr-evidence.test.mjs`: 89/89 tests passed.
- scoped Biome and `git diff --check`: passed.
- mutation proof: removing fenced-block blank-line preservation makes `keeps complete fenced transcripts intact across blank lines` fail because only the checkbox remains; restoring it returns the case and full suite to green.

2e:T197a,Closes #17610

**Following the repository's own two contribution documents always produced an invalid attribution marker.** The published ledger shows the scale: **3 valid against 64 missing-or-invalid of 67 eligible sources (95.5% failure)**, including PRs authored by @lalalune (#17424, #17555).

This changes **no scoring** — `leaderboard.ts:1379` states that complete, partial, missing, and invalid attribution states *"add no points."* What was broken is the **provenance dataset the attribution system exists to produce**: `attributions`, `attributionCoverage`, and `invalidAttributionMarkers` currently describe almost nothing that actually happened.

## Root cause

`markerFooterError` required **exactly one** of each visible row across the *entire body*. But `.github/pull_request_template.md:28-37` already ships three of those labels as checklist rows, and `SKILL.md` says *"complete every stable contribution-attribution row in the repository template **and append this footer after the template**"* — where the footer repeats them. (The skill-revision regex accepts both `Skill revision` and `Contribution skill revision`, so the template row and the footer row both count.) Every label appears twice, `length !== 1` fires, marker rejected — contributor did everything right.

## The fix

Count footer rows only within the **terminal attribution block**: the contiguous run of label lines ending at the already-validated lane signature. A checklist row ninety lines above the footer is not a competing footer. Duplicate detection is deliberately unweakened — rows abutting the lane are inside that block and still collide, and the at-most-one-marker gate is untouched.

## Proof

<details><summary>isolating the cause to a single line (real validator)</summary>

```
A. footer alone (no template rows):            valid=1 invalid=0
B. ONE template attribution row above footer:  valid=1 invalid=1  marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
C. the real template's 3 rows above footer:    valid=1 invalid=1  marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
```
</details>

<details><summary>end-to-end: real template + skill footer + the real evidence tool (before → after)</summary>

```
BEFORE
1. .github/pull_request_template.md + footer appended after it:  valid=1 invalid=1  marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
2. after patchRow on a template-present row:                     valid=1 invalid=1  (same)
3. after patchEvidenceHead + a row absent from the body:         valid=1 invalid=1  (same)

AFTER
1. .github/pull_request_template.md + footer appended after it:  valid=1 invalid=0
2. after patchRow on a template-present row:                     valid=1 invalid=0
3. after patchEvidenceHead + a row absent from the body:         valid=1 invalid=0
```
</details>

<details><summary>suite</summary>

```
$ bun test packages/eliza-computer/src/lib/leaderboard.test.ts
 54 pass
 0 fail
```
(52 pre-existing, unchanged, plus two new: the real template's rows above an appended footer validate; adjacent duplicated rows still fail with the original reason.)
</details>

## A second, related defect — reported, not fixed here

`scripts/pr-evidence.mjs` `patchRow:332-339` and `patchEvidenceHead:353-364` **append** when a marker is absent, which relocates content after a trailing attribution footer and trips the separate `"marker must be the final source content"` rule. I hit this on four of my own PRs and repaired them by hand; no instruction tells a contributor to re-order. It is left out of this PR to keep the diff to one concern — happy to send it as a follow-up making those functions insert *before* a trailing footer, if maintainers want it separately.

- Backend logs: the verbatim before/after transcripts above.
- Domain artifact: the three-case isolation showing a single template row flips validity.

30:Td8a,## Relates to

Repairs the three real failures in develop Scenario run 30842248728: view lifecycle, ratcheted chat performance, and deterministic aggregate.

## Summary

- synchronize Settings state with both `popstate` and `hashchange`
- coalesce streamed text and tool mutations at animation-frame cadence without dropping terminal or abort updates
- apply tail mutations directly through the production ref-backed chat state path
- invalidate stale scheduled callbacks across aborts, subsequent turns, and conversation switches
- drive the strict performance fixture through `useChatState`, `useChatSend`, `applyStreamingMessageModifications`, `useShellController`, and `ChatOverlay` rather than a synthetic message array
- cover abort/next-turn, stale frame and microtask callbacks, conversation switch/return, and multiple tool lifecycle events at settlement

## Root cause

The Settings view observed only hash navigation, while browser history traversal can update state through `popstate`. Streaming also committed every text/tool delta through broad React state updates, creating avoidable render work; the original performance fixture bypassed that production path and could not prove the repair.

## Risk

Medium. This changes the production streaming scheduler and tail-update path. Generation guards, synchronous terminal/abort flushes, conversation identity, and tool lifecycle ordering are covered explicitly.

## Verification

- focused Vitest: 99 passed, including Settings navigation and streaming lifecycle races
- strict production-pipeline perf gate: median p95 9.6 ms, 0% dropped frames, zero CLS, zero outside-chat reflow
- final CHOICE widget retained exactly once
- Biome and `git diff --check`: passed
- exact-head hosted Scenario: pending

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `origin/develop` at `7c267ad578ad964632cac0ca67f2710444deca5c`; zero conflicts.
- [x] `bun run verify` passes post-sync.

# Risks

Low. Retries are limited to three full GET attempts with 250 ms and 500 ms waits. The classifier is deliberately narrow: authentication and other non-transient failures still surface immediately, and malformed successful output is never retried or accepted.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Makes the contribution skill's GitHub inventory reads resilient to transient transport failures and GitHub HTTP 5xx responses. Each retry starts the paginated GET from the beginning, so partial output from a failed attempt cannot leak into the final report. Exhausted failures report the attempt count.

The regression suite covers TLS timeout recovery, HTTP 503 recovery, exhaustion, GET-only request semantics, ignored partial output, immediate authentication failure, spawn failure, and malformed successful JSON.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No. This preserves the documented CLI contract and changes only failure handling plus its regression coverage.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Run the focused contribution-skill test, then run the live report command shown in the evidence row. The live command must finish with a complete parsed inventory rather than a partial result.

33:T29c1,Closes #17597.

Adds a unit test for `sliceToFitBudget`, which had none.

## Why this function

It is the only thing bounding the `actionState` provider's action history before
that history reaches the model
([`actionState.ts:203`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/develop/packages/core/src/features/basic-capabilities/providers/actionState.ts#L203),
its sole caller, on the `fromEnd: true` path). A regression here throws nothing —
it either drops the agent's most recent run from context or lets the prompt slip
past `ACTION_HISTORY_TARGET_CHARS`. Both fail quietly.

## What is covered

16 cases across both directions: exact fit (the `>` vs `>=` boundary), first item
too large, the contiguous-slice guarantee, empty input, zero/negative budget,
zero-cost items, single-estimation, and input immutability.

## Proof the suite is load-bearing

A test file that merely restates the implementation passes forever and catches
nothing, so I mutated the real source and confirmed the suite fails. Each row
below is a separate run against a deliberately broken `slice-to-fit-budget.ts`:

| Mutation applied to the source | Suite result | First case to catch it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `> targetChars` → `>= targetChars` (exact-fit off-by-one) | 3 failed / 13 passed | `includes an item that exactly exhausts the budget` |
| `break` → `continue` (drops the contiguity guarantee) | 4 failed / 12 passed | `stops at the first item that does not fit rather than skipping it` |
| `targetChars <= 0` → `targetChars < 0` (guard weakened) | 1 failed / 15 passed | `treats a zero budget as no room even for a zero-cost item` |
| `break` → `continue` in the **`fromEnd`** loop | 2 failed / 14 passed | `returns a contiguous suffix, never a gap` (after @lalalune's fix — see below) |
| `if (items.length === 0) return [];` removed | 16 passed — **survived** | none, and correctly so |

The fourth is reported rather than hidden: that guard is a fast path, not a
behavior. With it gone the forward loop still yields `slice(0, 0)` and the
`fromEnd` loop still yields `slice(0)`, so the function is observably identical.
It is an equivalent mutant, and writing a test that pretended to kill it would be
the exact larp this suite exists to avoid.

The third mutation survived my first draft. Rather than leave the hole I added
the zero-cost-item case, which is what now closes it — the table above is the
re-run, not the original.

Source was restored and verified clean (`git diff --quiet`) after every mutation;
only the test file is added in this PR.

## Checks

`bun run --cwd packages/core test -- src/utils/slice-to-fit-budget.test.ts` →
16 passed. `bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck` → clean.
`bunx biome check` → clean.

Final maintainer replay: rebased without conflict onto `origin/develop@c9ebdf0e96e553f6a4c946c3dd3d78edeff4e9a2`; exact head `2a3a60c1fff37369a425f8ec9a744f9c83295674`. The 16-test suite, package typecheck, Biome, and diff hygiene all pass after the rebase.

The screenshot harness raced that state.

## Exact-head evidence

Evidence is bound to PR head `d561d2a30c38b981b7454348fbfa7f15c287ca42` and its parent `2711be746311f4e4e883b7afa38be2f11b6959a4`. - screenshots, walkthrough, logs, geometry, and OCR were manually reviewed

All three are directly covered by this PR's changed Linux baselines; `leaderboard-desktop` is the fourth changed baseline. This is coverage corroboration only, not current-develop proof.

## Readiness defect

**Keep this PR in draft.** Manual exact-head review found that `data-phone-model="settled"` fences camera completion but not completion of the chat canvas used by the leaderboard screenshot. At the declared boundary, desktop is missing the final response and mobile is missing the final two messages. The test passes because Playwright's screenshot assertion retries until a later frame. The [marker-frame comparison](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-2/17448-d561d2a-settled-marker-leaderboard-desktop-mobile.jpg) and [walkthrough](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-2/17448-d561d2a-walkthrough.mp4) show the defect directly.

Refs #17422

## Maintainer review\n\nThe submitted manifests requested Vite 8.2.0 while \x60bun.lock\x60 remained at 8.0.16, making frozen installs fail. Smartglasses also hard-coded \x60bunx --package vite@8.0.13\x60 in both real development commands, so its operational path ignored the manifest bump. The repair regenerates the lockfile, updates both smartglasses commands to 8.2.0, and rebases onto current develop. Frozen install, homepage production build, plugin-facewear build, the real smartglasses dev server/module transform, and Smartglasses Biome checks passed. Existing native-config and optimizeDeps migration warnings remain nonblocking debt.\n\n<!-- exact-head:f557bfe23346d9bb8f31b785d66a03110c0b2cc4 -->\n\n<!-- dependabot-policy:v1:start -->

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased without conflict onto `origin/develop@a38ea13ca8af98314b8ea3b494b3666a352f248e`.
- [x] Exact head: `6b3276b19525b4057701dce3cf8ceca6b42eaa33`.

# Background

`NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE` used `\b` boundaries. JavaScript treats punctuation in commands, paths, filenames, and member names as word boundaries, so a real transcript that merely named one of the gate's marker words could void the entire evidence row.

The boundary now requires a marker to stand apart from identifier punctuation on both sides. A dot is treated as identifier punctuation only when it starts an extension or member; sentence-ending punctuation still leaves a bare marker detectable:

```js
/(?<![\w/\\@:.-])(?:placeholder|…|mocks?|fixtures?|…)(?![\w/\\@-]|:[\w-]|\.[\w-])/i
```

This covers hyphenated commands, POSIX and Windows paths, colon-delimited script names, filenames, and object members. Explicit disclosure that evidence is fake, mocked, a placeholder, or otherwise non-real remains rejected.

## Why this is the implementation fix

The original patch protected only `/` and `-`. Maintainer review extended the rule and tests to cover `\\`, `.`, `:`, and `@`, while preserving sentence-ending punctuation. The full test suite now exercises the production evaluator, not only the embedded self-test.

# Testing

The load-bearing control is unchanged: reverting the boundary makes the marker-bearing identifier case fail. On the final rebased head:

```text
$ bun test scripts/check-pr-evidence.test.mjs
93 pass
0 fail
check-pr-evidence self-test passed (14 cases).

$ bunx biome check scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs scripts/check-pr-evidence.test.mjs
Checked 2 files. No fixes applied.

$ git diff --check origin/develop...HEAD
(exit 0)
```

The 93 cases include the merged complete-fence parser and fork-file verification suites, plus the new command/path/member boundary case.

38:T1d8c,Closes #17600

A fork contributor's real text evidence upload (`.log`/`.json`/`.jsonl`/…) mints `https://github.com/user-attachments/files/<id>/<name>` — a shape `trustedArtifact()` did not recognize, so the row reported **`blank`** exactly as if nothing were attached. The two document-capable shapes the gate did trust are both unreachable from a fork: the `pr-evidence` release path requires push access (`pr-evidence.mjs attach` 404s for non-collaborators), and `/user-attachments/assets/<uuid>` is only minted for media. The self-test fixture masked this by asserting `backend.txt` at an `/assets/` UUID — a shape GitHub never mints for text.

## The fix

- `trustedArtifact()` now recognizes `^/user-attachments/files/[0-9]+/[^/]+$` as a distinct **`file-upload`** kind, accepted **only** when the filename's extension is in `EVIDENCE_FILE_EXTENSIONS`. The files path carries a real extension (unlike the opaque assets path), so validation here is *stricter*: `.zip`, extension-less names, non-numeric ids, and nested paths stay untrusted (all covered red in tests).
- Deliberately **not** `opaque-upload`: visual rows treat linked opaque uploads as media, and a `.log` must never satisfy a walkthrough-video row — guarded by a new test.
- The existing byte-level document checks are unchanged and still run on fetch (readable text, placeholder markers, size floors). No new origin is trusted — `/user-attachments/files/` is the same authenticated GitHub upload surface as `/assets/`, with server-assigned immutable ids.
- Self-test fixture corrected to the `/files/` shape GitHub actually mints for `backend.txt`.

This composes with the pasted-transcript path (#17588): pasting covers short logs, but PR bodies cap at 65,536 characters and complete artifacts (full logs, real-LLM trajectory JSONL) routinely exceed inline limits — attachment is the only route at exactly the sizes where evidence matters most.

## Proof

**Live end-to-end, against the real fork-minted URL** (uploaded through the #17599 comment box, published in the #17600 repro comment):

<details><summary>before/after REPL transcript + live fetch verdict (verbatim)</summary>

```

  isRowSatisfied: false
AFTER (fix/evidence-trust-file-uploads):
  hasArtifactReference: true
  isRowSatisfied: true

"url":"https://github.com/user-attachments/files/30640882/17599-verification.log",

"contentSha256":"13a01ee7a90029019e8d778207e04d788419c3459952c826062eed60fd70ab40"}]}
```

(Operational note surfaced by this repro: a draft-minted attachment serves HTTP 404 to unauthenticated fetchers until its containing comment/body is actually posted — the gate's `artifact-http-error` on freshly minted URLs is that, not a bad upload.)

<!-- evidence-head:ea6a8223ff2356b3fda23268b98552b1e092a95c -->3a:Ta0a,Closes #17659.

## Summary

- materialize the CUDA link stub in the builder stage
- bind-mount the stub only while executing the existing `llama-server --version` build gate
- keep the stub out of the published runtime image
- add a focused contract test that forbids copying the driver stub into the final image

## Root cause

NVIDIA injects the real host driver library only when a container starts through the GPU runtime. BuildKit does not receive `libcuda.so.1`, so executing the CUDA-linked binary during image construction fails even when the image contains every redistributable dependency. The workflow has failed this way since its inaugural run; #17497 only triggered the existing path-filtered workflow.

The ephemeral link stub is stronger than merely inspecting `ldd`: it preserves the executable smoke test and therefore still catches broken binaries, bad links, and missing image-owned libraries without baking a fake driver into production.

## Verification

- focused Dockerfile contract: 1 test, 4 assertions passed
- contract requires the ephemeral cross-stage bind mount
- contract rejects copying `libcuda.so` into the final image
- Biome and `git diff --check`: passed
- full image build: unavailable locally because the Docker daemon is not running; exact hosted workflow is required before merge

<!-- evidence-head:eb4c6122dabf44d31f685739ed3e50959fcbc026 -->3c:T8a5,## Summary

Add `popstate` event listener alongside `hashchange` in `SettingsView` so same-page `history.pushState()` navigation (e.g. agent moving from `/settings#identity` to `/settings#ai-model`) correctly synchronizes the active section.

## Problem

- Added two regression tests covering same-page `pushState` + `popstate` and popstate to an unknown hash.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Four of the five `test:lint*` rows in the "Removed Root Command Migrations" table pointed at commands that do not exist. Following the table produced `error: Script not found`. This makes the rows accurate:

| Old command | Was | Now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `test:lint` | `audit:test-integrity:all` (absent) | `audit:test-integrity:no-vi-mocks` (only the no-vi-mocks check survives) |
| `test:lint:no-vi-mocks` | `audit:test-integrity:no-vi-mocks` | unchanged — this one existed |
| `test:lint:lane-coverage` | `audit:test-integrity:lane-coverage` (absent) | retired with its tooling; no replacement |
| `test:lint:test-integrity` | `audit:test-integrity` (absent) | retired with its tooling; no replacement |
| `test:lint:test-integrity:self-test` | `audit:test-integrity:self-test` (absent) | retired with its tooling; no replacement |

`AGENTS.md` receives the byte-identical edit, per the repo convention enforced by `check:agents-claude`.

## What kind of change is this?

Documentation only. No script, tooling, or behavior change.

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

These are not unregistered scripts — the tooling is gone: `packages/scripts/` contains `lint-no-vi-mocks.mjs` and no `test-integrity` or `lane-coverage` counterpart. So the honest fix is to say the checks were retired rather than to invent replacements.

The audience is what makes it worth a PR. This file is the repository guide **for agents**, and the migration table is the first thing consulted when a remembered command disappears. An agent that runs `bun run audit:test-integrity`, gets `Script not found`, and has no working alternative either substitutes something unverified or reports a blocked task — both worse than an accurate table. `CLAUDE.md` sets the same bar for itself: "A wrong header is worse than no header."

# Documentation changes needed?

This is the documentation change.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

The five `test:lint*` rows, then the resolution check below.

## Detailed testing steps

Every documented replacement in the table was resolved against the manifest that would actually run it — root targets against root `package.json`, `--cwd <dir>` targets against that package's own manifest, `node <path>` targets against the filesystem:

```
resolved 31/31 documented replacements
ALL ROWS RESOLVE
```

</details>

Before this change the same check reported four unresolved rows, all in the `test:lint*` family. Worth noting for scope: the `--cwd` rows (`packages/app` `test:e2e`, `packages/browser-extension` `test`, `packages/app-core` `voice:duet`, `packages/examples/smartglasses` `hardware:doctor`, …) all resolve correctly and are untouched.

# Checks run

Run on the final head, branched from `origin/develop` (`59b6f8b8`):

- per-row resolution of every documented replacement → **31/31 resolve**
- `bun run check:agents-claude` → PASS, 303 pairs byte-identical
- `bun run audit:test-integrity:no-vi-mocks` → the script executes correctly (see below)

`bun run verify` was **not** run in full: this changes two Markdown files and no TypeScript. Stating that rather than implying a green I did not produce.

# Two observations, explicitly out of scope

**1. The evidence gate rejects a real transcript that names this very command.** `NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE` in `scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs` treats the word `mocks` as a fabrication marker, so a genuine transcript quoting `audit:test-integrity:no-vi-mocks` is classified as non-real evidence and the row reads `blank`. That is why one word is redacted in the `domain-artifacts` block above — the row passes only with the command name removed, which is the opposite of what the gate wants. `fixtures`, `example output`, and `dummy` are in the same pattern and will misfire the same way on legitimate output. Raised separately rather than fixed here; happy to take it.

**2.

`audit:test-integrity:no-vi-mocks` — now the only surviving row in this family — **exits non-zero on `develop`**, reporting ~19,600 `vi.fn()` / `vi.spyOn()` occurrences across the test suite. The script itself works; the codebase simply has that much to report, so the check is effectively unenforced today.

I have deliberately not touched this here: this PR is about the table being accurate, and deciding whether that lint should be enforced, scoped, or retired like its siblings is a maintainer call. Flagging it because a reader following the corrected row will hit it immediately, and it seemed worse to leave that undiscovered.

45:T963,Closes #17661.

## Summary

- restore the four training dependency bounds represented by the validated `uv.lock`
- pin Stanza to the exact version required by Argos Translate
- replace Docker `uv sync --frozen` with `uv sync --locked` so metadata drift fails closed

## Root cause

Merged Dependabot PR #16531 changed four project constraints without updating the training lockfile. One change is unsatisfiable: `argostranslate==1.11.0` requires `stanza==1.10.1`, while the manifest requested 1.12.2. The wider Torch and vLLM bounds also no longer described the authenticated lock.

The Dockerfiles used `--frozen`, which skips freshness validation. `--locked` preserves reproducible installation while rejecting any future manifest and lock divergence.

## Verification

- reproduced the original unsatisfiable resolver failure
- `uv lock --check`: 319 packages resolved
- locked base sync dry run: 90 packages selected
- locked `train` export: complete 319-package graph
- `git diff --check`: passed
- Docker builds: unavailable locally because the Docker daemon is offline; hosted workflows must pass before merge

46:T9c7,## Relates to

Advances #13402 by removing the floating Bun runtime from the required Windows compatibility gate.

## Summary

- replace `bun-version: canary` in `windows-ci.yml` with the canonical `1.3.14` pin
- add `windows-ci.yml` to the CI Bun-version contract and mirrored test inventory
- keep exploratory canary coverage outside the deterministic required gate

## Root cause

Develop run 30851529785 used a different moving canary revision than the previous green run. Two unrelated Windows shards then failed through abrupt PGlite/Drizzle termination and shell-process timeout/EBUSY behavior; the failed-shard rerun already changed behavior without a source change. A required compatibility gate must test a reproducible toolchain, regardless of whether a rerun happens to pass.

## Risk

Low. This changes only the Bun installer input for the Windows workflow and expands an existing static drift guard. The canonical pin is already used across deterministic CI gates.

## Verification

- `bun test packages/scripts/__tests__/ci-bun-version-contract.test.ts packages/scripts/__tests__/windows-ci-workflow.test.ts`: 11 passed
- `node packages/scripts/ci-bun-version-contract.mjs`: canonical 1.3.14; 39 concrete pins; 10 gate lanes
- Biome and `git diff --check`: passed

<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/releases">undici's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>v8.9.0</h2>
<h2>⚠️ Security fixes</h2>
<h3>High severity</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-4cwx-7wf7-3272">GHSA-4cwx-7wf7-3272</a>: malformed qualified <code>private</code> Cache-Control directives could cause cross-user information disclosure in shared caches or a parse-time crash. The cache parser now treats empty qualified directives conservatively and safely handles mixed qualified and unqualified directives. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/4fe5bc5fefe5ac81a200fc8e1cf84b8bf8464451">4fe5bc5f</a> with regression coverage in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/9f09b49accd391cca818409447f2fb8bc93229b3">9f09b49a</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h3>Medium severity</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-m8rv-5g2x-5cg5">GHSA-m8rv-5g2x-5cg5</a>: a malicious <code>type</code> property on a duck-typed blob-like HTTP/1.1 request body could inject CRLF sequences into the generated <code>content-type</code> header. Undici now coerces and validates the value before adding it to the request. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/7d3cf924c262c486bc77f951348f4e5c847b7b42">7d3cf924</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-jr45-8vmc-qm54">GHSA-jr45-8vmc-qm54</a>: optional whitespace around <code>=</code> in qualified <code>no-cache</code> and <code>private</code> directives could bypass shared-cache restrictions and disclose authenticated data across users. Cache-Control parsing now normalizes these forms and applies conservative cache decisions. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/c601fff1c56eca84438c3ed4ecb39404252be622">c601fff1</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-8xcm-r25x-g524">GHSA-8xcm-r25x-g524</a>: the retry interceptor could expose a stale <code>Content-Length</code> after resuming a partial response, potentially causing downstream response desynchronization, hangs, or corruption. Undici now rejects partial responses whose <code>Content-Length</code> is inconsistent with <code>Content-Range</code>. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/e11a68ed4ff345c79402476f7a00d473443e318d">e11a68ed</a>, with corrected fixtures in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/2b3f749336d356bbbc50192f87f6cf7bc714721a">2b3f7493</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-v3r7-h72x-cjcm">GHSA-v3r7-h72x-cjcm</a>: unsanitized <code>domain</code> and <code>unparsed</code> values passed to <code>setCookie()</code> could inject cookie attributes. Undici now validates cookie domains, paths, and unparsed attributes more strictly. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/10d93fc332f2c8c161982dec3833201de29891b5">10d93fc3</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h2>Additional hardening</h2>
<p>Undici now validates non-string header values after coercion, including array elements, preventing crafted <code>toString()</code> or <code>Symbol.toPrimitive</code> implementations from introducing CRLF sequences. This defense-in-depth change was made in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/354a151f615f03d72f6aff02f3ad18a7e99a72b5">354a151f</a>.</p>

<blockquote>
<h2>v8.9.0</h2>
<h2>⚠️ Security fixes</h2>
<h3>High severity</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-4cwx-7wf7-3272">GHSA-4cwx-7wf7-3272</a>: malformed qualified <code>private</code> Cache-Control directives could cause cross-user information disclosure in shared caches or a parse-time crash. The cache parser now treats empty qualified directives conservatively and safely handles mixed qualified and unqualified directives. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/4fe5bc5fefe5ac81a200fc8e1cf84b8bf8464451">4fe5bc5f</a> with regression coverage in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/9f09b49accd391cca818409447f2fb8bc93229b3">9f09b49a</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h3>Medium severity</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-m8rv-5g2x-5cg5">GHSA-m8rv-5g2x-5cg5</a>: a malicious <code>type</code> property on a duck-typed blob-like HTTP/1.1 request body could inject CRLF sequences into the generated <code>content-type</code> header. Undici now coerces and validates the value before adding it to the request. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/7d3cf924c262c486bc77f951348f4e5c847b7b42">7d3cf924</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-jr45-8vmc-qm54">GHSA-jr45-8vmc-qm54</a>: optional whitespace around <code>=</code> in qualified <code>no-cache</code> and <code>private</code> directives could bypass shared-cache restrictions and disclose authenticated data across users. Cache-Control parsing now normalizes these forms and applies conservative cache decisions. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/c601fff1c56eca84438c3ed4ecb39404252be622">c601fff1</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-8xcm-r25x-g524">GHSA-8xcm-r25x-g524</a>: the retry interceptor could expose a stale <code>Content-Length</code> after resuming a partial response, potentially causing downstream response desynchronization, hangs, or corruption. Undici now rejects partial responses whose <code>Content-Length</code> is inconsistent with <code>Content-Range</code>. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/e11a68ed4ff345c79402476f7a00d473443e318d">e11a68ed</a>, with corrected fixtures in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/2b3f749336d356bbbc50192f87f6cf7bc714721a">2b3f7493</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-v3r7-h72x-cjcm">GHSA-v3r7-h72x-cjcm</a>: unsanitized <code>domain</code> and <code>unparsed</code> values passed to <code>setCookie()</code> could inject cookie attributes. Undici now validates cookie domains, paths, and unparsed attributes more strictly. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/10d93fc332f2c8c161982dec3833201de29891b5">10d93fc3</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h2>Additional hardening</h2>
<p>Undici now validates non-string header values after coercion, including array elements, preventing crafted <code>toString()</code> or <code>Symbol.toPrimitive</code> implementations from introducing CRLF sequences. This defense-in-depth change was made in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/354a151f615f03d72f6aff02f3ad18a7e99a72b5">354a151f</a>.</p>
<h2>What's Changed</h2>
<ul>
<li>fix(decompress): preserve response trailers by <a href="https://github.com/Ram-blip"><code>@​Ram-blip</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/nodejs/undici/pull/5572">nodejs/undici#5572</a></li>
<li>build(deps-dev): bump ws from 8.21.0 to 8.21.1 by <a href="https://github.com/dependabot"><code>@​dependabot</code></a>[bot] in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/nodejs/undici/pull/5576">nodejs/undici#5576</a></li>
<li>fix: validate coerced header values for CRLF by <a href="https://github.com/mcollina"><code>@​mcollina</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/nodejs/undici/pull/5579">nodejs/undici#5579</a></li>
<li>fix: handle frozen globalThis in setGlobalDispatcher by <a href="https://github.com/mhayk"><code>@​mhayk</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/nodejs/undici/pull/5574">nodejs/undici#5574</a></li>
<li>Ignore auto-generated .npmrc on Windows by <a href="https://github.com/tsctx"><code>@​tsctx</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/nodejs/undici/pull/5583">nodejs/undici#5583</a></li>

<blockquote>
<h2>v8.9.0</h2>
<h2>⚠️ Security fixes</h2>
<h3>High severity</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-4cwx-7wf7-3272">GHSA-4cwx-7wf7-3272</a>: malformed qualified <code>private</code> Cache-Control directives could cause cross-user information disclosure in shared caches or a parse-time crash. The cache parser now treats empty qualified directives conservatively and safely handles mixed qualified and unqualified directives. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/4fe5bc5fefe5ac81a200fc8e1cf84b8bf8464451">4fe5bc5f</a> with regression coverage in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/9f09b49accd391cca818409447f2fb8bc93229b3">9f09b49a</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h3>Medium severity</h3>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-m8rv-5g2x-5cg5">GHSA-m8rv-5g2x-5cg5</a>: a malicious <code>type</code> property on a duck-typed blob-like HTTP/1.1 request body could inject CRLF sequences into the generated <code>content-type</code> header. Undici now coerces and validates the value before adding it to the request. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/7d3cf924c262c486bc77f951348f4e5c847b7b42">7d3cf924</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-jr45-8vmc-qm54">GHSA-jr45-8vmc-qm54</a>: optional whitespace around <code>=</code> in qualified <code>no-cache</code> and <code>private</code> directives could bypass shared-cache restrictions and disclose authenticated data across users. Cache-Control parsing now normalizes these forms and applies conservative cache decisions. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/c601fff1c56eca84438c3ed4ecb39404252be622">c601fff1</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-8xcm-r25x-g524">GHSA-8xcm-r25x-g524</a>: the retry interceptor could expose a stale <code>Content-Length</code> after resuming a partial response, potentially causing downstream response desynchronization, hangs, or corruption. Undici now rejects partial responses whose <code>Content-Length</code> is inconsistent with <code>Content-Range</code>. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/e11a68ed4ff345c79402476f7a00d473443e318d">e11a68ed</a>, with corrected fixtures in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/2b3f749336d356bbbc50192f87f6cf7bc714721a">2b3f7493</a>.</li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-v3r7-h72x-cjcm">GHSA-v3r7-h72x-cjcm</a>: unsanitized <code>domain</code> and <code>unparsed</code> values passed to <code>setCookie()</code> could inject cookie attributes. Undici now validates cookie domains, paths, and unparsed attributes more strictly. Fixed by <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/10d93fc332f2c8c161982dec3833201de29891b5">10d93fc3</a>.</li>
</ul>
<h2>Additional hardening</h2>
<p>Undici now validates non-string header values after coercion, including array elements, preventing crafted <code>toString()</code> or <code>Symbol.toPrimitive</code> implementations from introducing CRLF sequences. This defense-in-depth change was made in <a href="https://github.com/nodejs/undici/commit/354a151f615f03d72f6aff02f3ad18a7e99a72b5">354a151f</a>.</p>

# Risks

Low. Adds one build-time generation step in the Docker pruner stage using an existing idempotent script already invoked by repo setup. Main risk is a missing script path or generator failure failing the image build early (fail-closed), which is preferred over a missing import at app build time.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Runs `packages/app-core/scripts/ensure-shared-i18n-data.mjs` in the `Dockerfile.ci` pruner stage immediately after `ensure-generated-core-proto-js.mjs`.
- Materializes gitignored shared/core `validation-keyword-data` TS/JS outputs so CI Docker builds can resolve `@elizaos/shared` keyword imports.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (CI / Docker build infrastructure, non-breaking).

## Relation to #17640

Open PR #17640 claims the same intent but its head **empties** `Dockerfile.ci` (0 additions / 246 deletions). This PR keeps the full Dockerfile and adds only the generation step.

# Documentation changes needed?

No product documentation change. Dockerfile comment documents why the step exists.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `packages/app-core/deploy/Dockerfile.ci` — the single new `RUN` after the proto ensure step.
2. Confirm `packages/app-core/scripts/ensure-shared-i18n-data.mjs` exists and invokes `packages/shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs`.

<!-- exact-head:e9fd9768c807f37cd6485a3bb7e56856cd3e4391 -->4a:T943,# fix(ci): generate shared i18n keyword data in Docker CI build

Closes #17468

## Summary

The `Dockerfile.ci` pruner stage only runs `ensure-generated-core-proto-js.mjs` but not `ensure-shared-i18n-data.mjs`. The app build imports `packages/shared/src/i18n/generated/validation-keyword-data.js`, which does not exist in a fresh checkout without this step.

Add the missing generation step to the pruner stage so the Docker build context always includes the generated keyword data files.

## Changes

- **`packages/app-core/deploy/Dockerfile.ci`**: Add `RUN node "${APP_CORE_DIR}/scripts/ensure-shared-i18n-data.mjs"` after the existing `ensure-generated-core-proto-js.mjs` step in the pruner stage. This ensures the generated i18n keyword data (.ts + .js) is present in the Docker build context before the app build runs.

## Why this works

The `ensure-shared-i18n-data.mjs` script already exists at `packages/app-core/scripts/ensure-shared-i18n-data.mjs` and is called by `run-repo-setup.mjs` during normal development. It runs `packages/shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs` which reads keyword JSON files and generates both TypeScript and JavaScript outputs. The Dockerfile.ci was simply missing this call.

## Acceptance criteria

- [x] The Docker build context always includes the generated keyword data files
- [x] The script `ensure-shared-i18n-data.mjs` already exists and is idempotent
- [x] No production behavior changes — this is a build-time fix only

## Evidence gate

|---|---|---|
| Screenshots / video | N/A | Build infrastructure change, no UI surface |
| Logs | N/A | Existing script output validates on re-run |
| Real-LLM trajectories | N/A | No model behavior changes |
| Domain artifacts | N/A | No runtime artifacts affected |
| Commands | `bun run build` in shared package validates keyword generation |

4b:T1479,# Overall Project Summary: Aug 3, 2026

Development on August 3 focused on hardening CI/CD infrastructure and strengthening cloud security boundaries to ensure deterministic builds and safer agent interactions. The team successfully stabilized the test matrix and improved configuration persistence, while ongoing efforts remain centered on resolving cross-platform e2e failures and unifying runtime environments.

## 🚨 Needs Attention 
- **[elizaos/eliza#16263](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/16263) Scheduled Android device e2e is failing**: Investigation is ongoing; contributors are transitioning to committed Bash harnesses to resolve emulator shell-boundary failures.
- **[elizaos/eliza#17043](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/17043) Unauthenticated loopback API callers**: Requires input on the implementation of a unified bearer-header helper to secure internal fetch calls.
- **[elizaos/eliza#17044](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/17044) Unify published Bun runtime**: High-priority task requiring alignment of all repository runtime declarations to `1.3.14`.

## ✅ Completed Work

**CI/CD Infrastructure and Stability**
*   Restored the develop test matrix and pinned training stack dependencies to ensure deterministic builds ([elizaos/eliza#17615](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17615), [elizaos/eliza#17637](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17637)).
*   Isolated fork quality jobs from runner state and pinned Bun runtimes to canonical versions ([elizaos/eliza#17523](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17523), [elizaos/eliza#17653](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17653)).
*   Improved visual readiness and test contract stability for homepage and benchmark workflows ([elizaos/eliza#17608](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17608), [elizaos/eliza#17639](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17639)).

**Agent Runtime and Security Enhancements**
*   Strengthened cloud security by rejecting caller-authored system policies at the A2A boundary and separating API/callback failure propagation ([elizaos/eliza#17643](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17643), [elizaos/eliza#17498](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17498)).
*   Enhanced agent configuration persistence by treating bind-mount overlays as authoritative snapshots ([elizaos/eliza#17648](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17648), [elizaos/eliza#17642](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17642)).
*   Optimized prompt cache reuse for Cerebras and added test coverage for action budget management ([elizaos/eliza#17515](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17515), [elizaos/eliza#17598](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/17598)).

## Scope

The contributor inventory aborts the entire report when any one paginated GET made by `readGhPages` hits a transient transport or GitHub 5xx failure. During a real inventory on 2026-08-03, three clean attempts stopped at different issue-comment endpoints with `net/http: TLS handshake timeout`; a temporary GET-only retry wrapper then completed the same report.

Add bounded retry/backoff inside the trusted read-only adapter. Keep the command fixed to `gh api --method GET`, keep malformed successful output fail-fast, and do not retry authentication, authorization, validation, or other non-transient failures.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Known transient transport failures and retryable GitHub 5xx responses are retried a small, bounded number of times with bounded backoff.
- [ ] A later successful attempt returns the complete paginated result without partial output.
- [ ] Exhausted transient failures report the endpoint and attempt count, while non-transient failures fail immediately.
- [ ] Spawn errors and malformed successful JSON remain immediate failures.
- [ ] Deterministic tests cover immediate success, transient recovery, exhaustion, non-transient rejection, and the invariant that every attempt is GET-only.
- [ ] The real read-only inventory completes against `elizaOS/eliza`, and the resulting JSON is opened and manually inspected.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: N/A - CLI reliability change with no rendered UI.
- [ ] Logs: attach the pre-fix TLS failure and post-fix live inventory transcript inline.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - no model, prompt, provider, action, or agent behavior changes.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: attach the post-fix machine-readable inventory summary and manually reviewed candidate excerpt.
- [ ] CI or local commands: focused package test, package build/typecheck/lint checks, `bun run verify`, and the live `--json` inventory.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"openai","model":"gpt-5.6-sol","client":"Codex desktop","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@4b0bad53a7fd5cc0c1f0c507c849f27ebb53b1d3:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->56:T1500,## Summary

`scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs` cannot be satisfied with a **trusted document file** by a fork contributor, because the two document-capable URL shapes it trusts are both unreachable from a fork, and the gate's own self-test fixture encodes an upload shape GitHub does not actually mint for text.

This is adjacent to but distinct from #17588 (help text omits the pasted-transcript path — that path works and is what forks must fall back to) and #16991 (head binding).

## Reproduced on `develop` (`9bdcf068b`) — all three legs

**1. Text uploads mint the untrusted `/files/` path.** Uploading `17599-verification.log` through the PR comment-box uploader mints:

```
https://github.com/user-attachments/files/30640882/17599-verification.log
```

`trustedArtifact()` recognizes only `^/user-attachments/assets/<uuid>$` (plus legacy repo assets and the `pr-evidence` release path) — `/user-attachments/files/…` returns `null`, so a row citing it reports **`blank`**, the same as if nothing were attached. GitHub's uploader mints `/assets/<uuid>` for media (png/jpg/mp4/mov/webm…) and `/files/<id>/<name>` for exactly the text formats the gate's `EVIDENCE_FILE_EXTENSIONS` set names (`.log`, `.txt`, `.json`, `.jsonl`, `.csv`, `.md`, `.html`).

**2. The release path 404s for forks.** `node scripts/pr-evidence.mjs rows <pr> --row backend-logs=<local file>` tries to upload to the `pr-evidence` release and fails for non-collaborators:

```
HTTP 404: Not Found (https://uploads.github.com/repos/elizaOS/eliza/releases/363485569/assets?label=&name=17599-bun-runtime-inventory.json)
```

Release-asset upload requires push access, so the documented `attach` flow is maintainer/fleet-only.

**3. The self-test fixture asserts a shape GitHub never mints.** The in-file self-test builds:

```
- [ ] Backend logs: [backend.txt](https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001)
```

— a **text file at an `/assets/` UUID**. Real uploads of `backend.txt` mint `/files/…`, so the fixture green-lights an upload path that does not exist, which is why this gap ships silently.

## Impact

- A fork contributor can satisfy non-visual rows only by pasting inline (`hasInlineTranscriptEvidence`) or writing `N/A`. Pasting stops working exactly where attachment matters most: PR bodies cap at 65,536 characters, and complete real artifacts — full backend logs, real-LLM trajectory JSONL (`hasSubstantiveInlineTrajectory` requires complete JSON/JSONL records inline) — routinely exceed what fits. The Definition of Done says "attach complete, real evidence"; for forks the gate structurally forbids the attachment it demands.
- Observed concretely on #17599: `backend-logs` had to ship as a pasted transcript because the real `.log` upload was classed untrusted.

## Proposed fix (I am implementing this now — PR to follow in this session)

Trust the second first-party upload shape, with tighter validation than `/assets/` gets:

1. Add `USER_ATTACHMENT_FILE_PATH_RE = ^/user-attachments/files/[0-9]+/[^/]+$` to `trustedArtifact()`, accepted **only** when `extensionFromPath()` is in `EVIDENCE_FILE_EXTENSIONS` (the filename carries a real extension here, unlike the extensionless `/assets/` UUIDs — so `.zip`/binaries stay rejected).
2. `expectedArtifactKind()` maps those to `document`; the existing byte-level document checks (readable text, `NON_REAL_DOCUMENT_RE`, size floors) still run unchanged.
3. Correct the self-test fixture to use the `/files/` shape for `backend.txt` and keep a media fixture on `/assets/`; add red tests for a `/files/` URL with a disallowed extension and for the previously-blank classification.

No new origin is trusted: `/user-attachments/files/` is the same authenticated GitHub upload surface as `/user-attachments/assets/`, with server-assigned immutable ids.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A `backend-logs` row citing a real `github.com/user-attachments/files/<id>/<name>.log` upload passes the gate (verified against a live minted URL, not a synthetic one).
- [ ] `/user-attachments/files/` URLs with non-evidence extensions (e.g. `.zip`, `.exe`-style names) remain untrusted; media rows still demand real media.
- [ ] The self-test fixture no longer asserts text-at-`/assets/`; red tests cover the disallowed-extension and untrusted-shape cases.
- [ ] `node scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs --self-test` (or the repo's equivalent invocation) and the script's test file pass; the gate's behavior for maintainer release uploads and pasted transcripts is unchanged.

## Evidence plan

- Logs: live minted `/files/` URL + `trustedArtifact()` REPL transcript before/after; full test run.
- Screenshots/video: N/A — validator policy change, no UI.
- Real-LLM trajectories: N/A — no model behavior.
- Domain artifact: the gate verdict diff (blank → ok) on a real fork-minted URL.

57:T1109,## Scope

`scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs` accepts **four** ways to satisfy a non-visual evidence row, but its failure help advertises only two. Authors who did the right thing — pasted a real transcript, exactly as `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Evidence prescribes — are told they need something else.

The gate's failure output ends with:

> Rules: visual rows on UI-touching PRs need REAL media (an uploaded image/video, not a link to the PR or /checks page); every other row needs an artifact link or 'N/A - <reason>'.

The implementation is broader. `isRowSatisfied` is also satisfied by `hasInlineTranscriptEvidence`, whose own comment states the reasoning:

> CONTRIBUTING.md § Evidence and the root AGENTS.md both prescribe "long logs in a `<details>` block", so a row that follows the documented standard must satisfy the gate — requiring a URL for every non-visual row contradicts the standard the gate cites and reports a real pasted transcript as `blank`.

So the code deliberately supports the pasted-transcript path, and the help text omits it.

Verified against `origin/develop` (`725b43ea`):

```js
import * as gate from "./scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs";

const pasted = `- [x] Backend logs:
\`\`\`
[develop-pr-gate] poll 1: passed=0 waiting=3 failed=0
[develop-pr-gate] poll 2: passed=2 waiting=1 failed=0
[develop-pr-gate] poll 3: passed=3 waiting=0 failed=0
[develop-pr-gate] all required checks green after 92s
\`\`\``;

gate.isRowSatisfied(pasted);   // true  — 243 chars, 4 lines
```

Shorten that same transcript below the `INLINE_TRANSCRIPT_MIN_CHARS = 120` / `INLINE_TRANSCRIPT_MIN_LINES = 3` thresholds and it returns `false`, with no message explaining which threshold was missed.

## Why this matters

Two bad outcomes, both of which lose information:

1. An author with a real log reads "needs an artifact link or N/A", uploads a file to the `pr-evidence` release that did not need to exist, or
2. — worse — marks the row `N/A - <reason>` and the genuine evidence never lands in the PR at all.

The second is a quality regression the gate exists to prevent. It also disproportionately affects outside contributors, who cannot upload release assets at all (no write access), so `N/A` looks like their only option.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The gate's failure help lists the inline-transcript path alongside artifact links and `N/A - <reason>`, and states the `INLINE_TRANSCRIPT_MIN_LINES` / `INLINE_TRANSCRIPT_MIN_CHARS` thresholds.

58:T1a17,## Project / Coordination

- Project board: N/A - runner-fleet reliability defect found while independently reviewing #17544.
- Parent tracker or epic: relates to #15398 (cleanup bundle) and #17534 / #17544 (control-dir protection).
- Coordination Discussion: N/A.
- Suggested lane tag: claude-code-ss251

## Scope

`packages/deploy/runner-workspace-cleanup/bin/eliza-prune-idle-runner-workspaces.sh` **fails open on a destructive path**: when it cannot determine which systemd unit owns a runner directory, it classifies that runner as *orphaned* and prunes its `_work` tree with `--allow-active`, which deliberately disables the tool's own safety guard.

The script is explicit that a miss is not an error:

```bash
# Every stage tolerates a miss: no match means orphaned, not an error.
agent_name=""
if [[ -r "${runner_dir}.runner" ]]; then
  agent_name="$(sed -n 's/.*"agentName"...' "${runner_dir}.runner" | head -1 || true)"
fi
unit=""
if [[ -n "$agent_name" ]]; then ... fi
if [[ -z "$unit" ]]; then
  echo "prune $name — no runner unit resolves to it (orphaned)"
fi
```

Tolerating a miss is right for the case the comment is defending (a genuinely deregistered runner, #15398). The defect is that **"absent" and "unreadable/unparseable" collapse into the same branch**, and that branch deletes.

### Defect 1 — unreadable or unparseable `.runner` reads as orphaned

`[[ -r ... ]]` failing, a transient I/O error, a `.runner` being rewritten during registration or a runner self-update, or any schema change to `agentName` all yield `agent_name=""` → `unit=""` → **prune with `--allow-active` on a runner that may be mid-job**. There is no distinction between "this runner does not exist" and "I could not tell".

### Defect 2 — `grep -F` substring match can resolve the wrong unit

```bash
| sed 's/^actions\.runner\.//' | grep -F -- "$agent_name" | head -1
```

`grep -F` is an unanchored substring match, so agent `runner-1` matches unit `…runner-10` first (`head -1` over `list-units` order). The script then consults **the wrong unit's** `is-active` state: if `runner-10` happens to be inactive while `runner-1` is running a job, `runner-1` is pruned as inactive. Both halves of the guard — the active check and the orphan check — are defeated by the same mismatch.

### Why this matters now

`--allow-active` is passed on this path by design (the comment explains why the tool's host-global `pgrep` guard is the wrong scope under `ProtectProc=invisible`). That is defensible **only if** the per-runner proof above is sound. Under either defect the proof is unsound and the last safety net is gone.

Observed consequences on `eliza-robot-6` this week — live jobs losing files mid-run:

- `Type Check`, job [91356781391](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30694519256/job/91356781391) (run 30694519256, `quality-fork.yml`): died **4 s into `actions/checkout`** with `Missing file at path: /opt/actions-runners/runner-6/_work/_temp/_runner_file_commands/save_state_231cd9bf-d25a-4158-826f-06df318efd1f`; every later step including `Run typecheck` was `skipped`. The PR was never type-checked, and the red X had nothing to do with its diff.
- #17534 cites two further runs (30692175805, 30691649390) with the same signature.

#17544 removes the **blast radius** by protecting `_temp`/`_actions`/`_tool`/`_update`/`_PipelineMapping`, and it should land. It does not address the **misclassification**: after it, a live runner mistaken for orphaned still has its repository checkouts deleted mid-job. That is this issue.

Note this also falsifies an unconditional claim in `packages/deploy/runner-workspace-cleanup/README.md` ("Never interrupting a live job — two halves, both race-free").

## Required behavior

- Distinguish *absent* from *undeterminable*. Only a runner with no `.runner` at all — or one whose unit demonstrably does not exist — may be treated as orphaned.
- On an unreadable or unparseable `.runner`, **skip and count it**, do not prune. Fail closed on the destructive branch while keeping the sweep going for other runners (the `set -e` lesson from #15398 stands).
- Resolve the unit by exact match on the unit suffix, not an unanchored substring, so `runner-1` can never resolve to `runner-10`.
- Preserve current behavior for genuine orphans, and keep per-runner error isolation and the exit-code accounting.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A runner directory whose `.runner` exists but is unreadable or unparseable is **not** pruned; it is reported and counted as undeterminable.
- [ ] A runner whose agentName is a strict prefix/substring of another runner's (`runner-1` vs `runner-10`) resolves to its own unit; a running `runner-1` is never pruned because `runner-10` is inactive.
- [ ] Genuine orphans (no `.runner`, or a unit that truly does not exist) are still pruned — the #15398 multi-orphan sweep still reports `pruned=2`.
- [ ] Both new behaviors are covered in `packages/deploy/runner-workspace-cleanup/smoke-test.sh`, driven through the **real** helper and **real** tool with only `systemctl` stubbed, matching the existing §10 pattern.
- [ ] Demonstrated red/green: with the fix reverted the new assertions fail; with it applied the whole smoke test passes. Both outputs attached.
- [ ] `README.md`'s race-freedom claim is amended to state the conditions under which it holds.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: N/A - shell/CI infrastructure with no rendered surface.
- [ ] Logs: full `smoke-test.sh` output before the fix (failing new assertions) and after (passing), plus the helper's sweep log showing the undeterminable runner skipped and the genuine orphan pruned in the same run.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - no model, prompt, or agent behavior involved.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: the temporary runner tree after the sweep, showing the live runner's `_work` intact and the orphan's reclaimed.
- [ ] CI or local commands: `bash packages/deploy/runner-workspace-cleanup/smoke-test.sh`.

5b:T1206,## Summary

A turn abort that lands **after every provider has resolved** is silently swallowed by `composeState`: the abort guard only fires when the abort caused at least one provider failure, so a late abort still publishes `stateCache` and returns a completed `State` — violating the ack-and-stop contract the guard's own comment (#16939) declares.

I am implementing this (file-and-fix); the PR will be coordinated with #17508's landing since that PR rewrites the same region (see coordination note below).

## Reproduced on `develop` (`9bdcf068b`) — and it survives on the #17508 head

`packages/core/src/runtime.ts` (develop), the guard:

```ts
// :4961
if (
  failedProviderData.length > 0 &&                 // ← requires a casualty
  providerSignal?.aborted &&
  failedProviderData.every((r) => r.providerOutcome === "aborted")
) {
  throw … TurnAbortedError …
}
```

Its own comment states the invariant: *"surface the abort itself so the message boundary keeps its ack-and-stop contract instead of emitting a canned runtime-failure apology (#16939)"*. But the `failedProviderData.length > 0` precondition means the invariant only holds when the abort arrived **early enough to kill a provider**. The race window:

1. All providers settle successfully (`failedProviderData` is empty).
2. The user taps "stop" / the client disconnects → `providerSignal` flips aborted.
3. The guard at `:4961` is skipped (no failures), aggregation proceeds, and `:5064` runs `this.stateCache.set(message.id, newState)` — no abort check exists anywhere between the last settlement and publication or between publication and `return`.

Result: the cancelled turn (a) publishes state that a later turn for the same `message.id` will read as legitimately cached, and (b) resolves `composeState` normally, so the caller continues acting on a turn the user stopped — exactly the #16939 failure shape, one window later.

On the #17508 head (`ebea48b`) the same publication-without-guard exists at its `:4946-4947`; re-derived in https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/17508#issuecomment-5150514957, with @lalalune independently reproducing the same item on the prior head (2026-08-01T08:49Z). Architecture-independent.

## Repair proposal

Two checks, symmetric with the intent the #16939 guard already declares:

1. Immediately before `stateCache.set`: `if (providerSignal?.aborted) throw providerSignal.reason instanceof TurnAbortedError ? providerSignal.reason : new TurnAbortedError(…)`.
2. Drop the `failedProviderData.length > 0` precondition from the existing guard (an aborted signal with zero casualties is still an aborted turn), or fold both into one post-settlement check.

Note `[].every(...)` is vacuously `true`, so removing the length precondition alone is behavior-correct for the all-resolved case.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] An abort raised after all providers resolve makes `composeState` throw `TurnAbortedError` (deterministic unit test: resolve all providers, then abort, then observe).
- [ ] No `stateCache` entry is written for that aborted turn; a subsequent `composeState` for the same `message.id` recomputes rather than reading poisoned cache.
- [ ] The early-abort path (providers killed mid-flight) behaves exactly as today (#16939 regression test stays green).
- [ ] `bun run --cwd packages/core test` green.

## Evidence plan

- Real-LLM trajectories: N/A — deterministic runtime lifecycle behavior; deterministic unit tests drive the real `composeState` path.
- Logs: failing test on current develop (state published + normal resolution after late abort), passing after; full test-lane run.
- Screenshots/video: N/A — no UI.
- Domain artifact: before/after `stateCache` contents for the aborted `message.id`, from the test.

## Coordination note

#17508 rewrites this region. If it lands first, the fix targets its shape (same two checks around its `:4946` publication site); if this lands first, the change is two small guards that rebase cleanly. The failing tests assert the contract ("late abort must surface, must not publish"), not the implementation.

5d:T112f,## Scope

`NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE` in `scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs` voids a whole evidence block when a fabrication marker appears anywhere inside it, including as part of an identifier. A genuine transcript that merely *names* a command or file containing one of those words is classified as fake evidence and the row reports `blank`.

The trigger is a real one from this repo:

```js
const NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE =
  /\b(?:placeholder|example output|logs? here|todo|tbd|fabricated|invented|fake|mocks?|fixtures?|synthetic|dummy)\b/i;

NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE.test("$ bun run audit:test-integrity:no-vi-mocks");   // true
NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE.test("packages/scripts/lint-no-vi-mocks.mjs:12");     // true
NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE.test("running fixtures/setup.ts");                    // true
```

`\b` matches after a hyphen and after a slash, so `no-vi-mocks` and `fixtures/setup.ts` both trip it. Any PR pasting real output from that lint — or from anything under a `fixtures/` path — has its evidence row rejected as fabricated.

I hit this while filling the evidence rows on #17591: the honest transcript quoted `audit:test-integrity:no-vi-mocks`, so the gate marked the row `blank`. I shipped that PR with one word redacted and a note explaining why, which is precisely the wrong incentive — the gate pushed real evidence out of the PR.

## Proposed fix

Require the marker to stand alone rather than appear inside a hyphenated or slashed identifier:

```js
const NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE =
  /(?<![\w\/-])(?:placeholder|example output|logs? here|todo|tbd|fabricated|invented|fake|mocks?|fixtures?|synthetic|dummy)(?![\w\/-])/i;
```

Verified against both directions — real evidence must pass, genuine fabrication must still be caught:

| Input | Current | Proposed | Wanted |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `$ bun run audit:test-integrity:no-vi-mocks` | flagged | passes | passes |
| `packages/scripts/lint-no-vi-mocks.mjs:12` | flagged | passes | passes |
| `running fixtures/setup.ts` | flagged | passes | passes |
| `TODO: logs here` | flagged | flagged | flagged |
| `this is a placeholder` | flagged | flagged | flagged |
| `output was fabricated` | flagged | flagged | flagged |
| `uses mocks instead of the real service` | flagged | flagged | flagged |

The last row matters: the point of `mocks`/`fixtures` in this pattern is to reject evidence produced by a mocked test, per the repo's "no larp" standard. That intent is preserved — a bare `mocks` in prose is still caught. Only occurrences welded into an identifier stop matching.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A transcript quoting a command or path containing a marker word (`no-vi-mocks`, `fixtures/…`) satisfies the gate when it otherwise meets the inline-transcript floor.
- [ ] Genuine fabrication markers still void the block: bare `placeholder`, `TODO`, `logs here`, `fabricated`, and a prose disclosure such as "uses mocks instead of the real service".
- [ ] `runSelfTest` covers both directions, so a future tightening cannot silently reintroduce the false positive.
- [ ] No other change to what the gate accepts.

## Evidence plan

- [ ] Screenshots / video: N/A - CI script change with no rendered surface.
- [ ] Logs: before/after classification for the table above, pasted inline.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - no model, prompt, action, or provider behavior changes.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: N/A - the self-test result is the artifact.
- [ ] Commands: `node scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs --self-test`, `node --test scripts/check-pr-evidence.test.mjs`.

## Blockers

None. One coordination note: #17589 also touches this file (help text plus a diagnostic, no acceptance change). This issue is deliberately separate because it *does* change what the gate accepts, and I did not want to widen a PR whose stated scope excluded that. Happy to sequence them in whichever order you prefer.
