## Evidence

Failure: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30793248114

The failed run reported missing huggingface_hub, httpx, AgentBench pinned data, and the official Hermes corpus.60:T1de5,## Summary

**The repository's PR template and the contribution skill are mutually incompatible: following both — as both documents instruct — always produces an invalid attribution marker.** The published ledger shows the result: of 67 eligible sources, **3 are valid and 64 are missing or invalid (95.5% failure)**, including PRs authored by @lalalune (#17424, #17555).

This costs **zero points** — `leaderboard.ts:1379` states plainly that complete, partial, missing, and invalid attribution states *"add no points."* I am not filing this for score. It breaks the **provenance dataset itself**, which is the stated purpose of the whole attribution system: the public `attributions`, `attributionCoverage`, and `invalidAttributionMarkers` fields currently describe almost nothing that actually happened.

I am implementing this (file-and-fix); PR follows in this session.

## Root cause

`markerFooterError` (`packages/eliza-computer/src/lib/leaderboard.ts:1035-1046`) requires **exactly one** of each visible row across the *entire body*:

```ts
const providerModelLines = attributionLineValues(body, "AI provider/model");
const clientLines        = attributionLineValues(body, "Client / agent tooling");
const skillRevisionLines = attributionLineValues(body, "(?:Contribution skill revision|Skill revision)");
const statusLines        = attributionLineValues(body, "Attribution status");
if (providerModelLines.length !== 1 || clientLines.length !== 1 ||
    skillRevisionLines.length !== 1 || statusLines.length !== 1) {
  return "marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer";
}
```

But `.github/pull_request_template.md:28-37` already ships three of those exact labels as checklist rows:

```markdown
<!-- attribution-row:client -->
- Client / agent tooling: `client-name` / `None - human-only contribution`
<!-- attribution-row:skill-revision -->
- Skill revision: `owner/repo@full-commit-sha:path` / `N/A - no contribution skill used`
<!-- attribution-row:status -->
- Attribution status: `self-reported`
```

…and `SKILL.md` instructs: *"In a PR body, complete every stable contribution-attribution row in the repository template **and append this footer after the template**"* — where the footer repeats `Client / agent tooling`, `Contribution skill revision`, and `Attribution status`. Note the skill-revision regex accepts **both** spellings, so the template's `Skill revision` row and the footer's `Contribution skill revision` row both count.

Every label therefore appears twice, `length !== 1` fires, and the marker is rejected. The contributor did everything both documents asked.

## Executable repro

Real validator, real template, no mocks — three cases, isolating the cause to a single line:

```ts
import { assessModelAttribution } from "packages/eliza-computer/src/lib/leaderboard.ts";
// FOOT(extra) = `extra` + the exact 5-line footer from SKILL.md + the v1 marker
run(FOOT(""),                                          "A. footer alone");
run(FOOT("- Attribution status: `self-reported`\n\n"), "B. ONE template row above the footer");
run(FOOT(templateClientSkillStatusRows),               "C. the real template's 3 rows above the footer");
```

```
A. footer alone (no template rows):
   valid=1 invalid=0
B. ONE template attribution row above the footer:
   valid=1 invalid=1 marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
C. the real template's 3 rows above the footer:
   valid=1 invalid=1 marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
```

And end-to-end with the shipped template plus the skill's prescribed footer — the exact body a compliant contributor produces:

```
1. .github/pull_request_template.md + footer appended after it (as SKILL.md instructs):
   valid=1 invalid=1 marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
```

The `visible-declaration` is parsed fine in every case; it is the **marker** that is rejected, which is why the ledger shows a valid declaration alongside an invalid marker for the same source.

## Second, independent defect: `scripts/pr-evidence.mjs` breaks marker finality

`markerFooterError:1031` also requires `body.slice(markerRecord.end).trim()` to be empty — the marker must be the final content. But `patchRow` (`scripts/pr-evidence.mjs:332-339`) appends when a row marker is absent:

```js
if (at === -1) {
  // Row marker absent (old template) — append a fresh marker + row.
  return `${body.trimEnd()}\n\n${marker}\n${line}\n`;
}
```

`patchEvidenceHead:353-364` appends the same way when there is no `# Evidence Gate` heading. So the official evidence tool, run against a body whose footer is last (exactly where SKILL.md puts it), relocates content **after** the marker and invalidates it — the `"marker must be the final source content"` reason. I hit this on four of my own PRs before catching it; the fix on my side was manual re-ordering, which no instruction tells a contributor to do.

## Proposed fix

1. **Scope the footer scan to the terminal declaration block** rather than the whole body. The rule's intent is "exactly one *footer*", not "exactly one occurrence of each label anywhere in the document" — a checklist row 90 lines above the footer is not a competing footer. Concretely: collect the contiguous run of declaration lines ending at the validated terminal lane signature, and count labels only within it. This keeps every genuinely-duplicated footer rejected, keeps footer-only bodies valid (no regression for the current 3), and makes template + footer valid — which is what both documents already tell contributors to produce.
2. **Make `pr-evidence.mjs` footer-aware**: when appending a fresh row marker or head marker, insert **before** a trailing attribution footer instead of after it, so the official tools compose.
3. Tests for both: template+footer valid; two complete footers still invalid; footer-only still valid; `patchRow`/`patchEvidenceHead` preserve marker finality.

Alternative considered and rejected as worse: renaming the template rows to avoid collision. It would fix new PRs while leaving the skill's "append after the template" instruction producing near-miss bodies, and it churns a file every contributor sees.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A body built from `.github/pull_request_template.md` + the SKILL.md footer validates with `valid=1 invalid=0` (executable test, real template).
- [ ] Two complete attribution footers in one body still fail with the existing reason (no weakening).
- [ ] A footer-only body still validates (no regression against the currently-valid sources).
- [ ] `patchRow` and `patchEvidenceHead` leave the attribution marker as the final content when one is present; covered red/green.
- [ ] `bun test packages/eliza-computer` and the `scripts/pr-evidence` tests pass.

## Evidence plan

- Logs: the three-case repro above, before/after the fix, plus the full test runs.
- Domain artifact: recomputed attribution coverage over the current ledger's source bodies, before vs after.
- Screenshots/video: N/A — validator and CLI logic, no UI.
- Real-LLM trajectories: N/A — no model behavior.

61:T122c,## Summary

The i18n source catalog has drifted **705 keys behind source**, because the strict checker that exists to prevent exactly this — `packages/scripts/check-i18n.mjs` — is wired into **no CI lane, no package script, nothing** (verified: zero references outside the file itself). English users never see it (`defaultValue` masks every miss), which is why it rotted silently; every non-English locale renders English for those 705 strings **and can never translate them**, because `en.json` is the catalog translators/locale files key off.

I am implementing this (file-and-fix); PR follows in this session.

## Reproduced on `develop` (`9bdcf068b`)

```
$ node packages/scripts/check-i18n.mjs; echo exit=$?
[i18n] en.json missing 705 key(s) used in source:
  - cloud.account.securityLink   (packages/ui/src/cloud/account-security/AccountPage.tsx:37)
  …
exit=1
```

```
$ grep -rn "check-i18n" package.json packages/*/package.json .github/workflows/*.yml
(no results — the checker is invoked from nowhere)
```

Independent rescan with the checker's own regexes over its own scan dirs: **4,519 used literal keys; 705 missing from `en.json`; 703 of those carry an inline English `defaultValue` at their call site** (zero conflicting defaults across duplicate sites); the remaining 2 (`settings.cloudOverview.localModeLabel`/`localModeDescription`) pass **conditional** defaults (ternaries on cloud-connection state), so cataloging them would freeze one branch and silently change runtime English — they need a key-split in the component, which is a UI-surface change deliberately out of scope here.

Non-source locales additionally lag the catalog (es/ja 824, ko 757 missing including the 705) — with the checker's current contract that makes it permanently red and therefore unwireable, which is presumably how it ended up wired nowhere.

## Proposed fix

1. **Restore the source catalog mechanically**: add the 703 missing entries to `en.json`, each value extracted verbatim from its call site's inline `defaultValue` — no invented copy, byte-for-byte what English users already see.
2. **Make the checker wireable, then wire it**: source-locale completeness, unused keys, and orphaned translations stay **errors**; non-source untranslated counts become a **warning summary** (full red available behind `--strict-translations`). Backfilling ~6,000 machine translations in a fix PR would be unreviewable; the durable invariant CI must hold is *no new key ships uncataloged*.
3. **`uncatalogued` allowlist field** for the 2 conditional-default keys, with the rationale in the JSON — visible debt, not silence.
4. **Wire the checker into the `test.yml` contract block** so it runs on every PR, and add red/green tests for the checker itself (missing-source error, warning-vs-strict translations, unused key, orphaned translation, uncatalogued handling) following the `ci-bun-version-contract` test pattern.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `node packages/scripts/check-i18n.mjs` exits 0 on the fixed tree; every added `en.json` value is byte-identical to the inline `defaultValue` at its call site (spot-checkable from the extraction manifest attached to the PR).
- [ ] The checker runs in `test.yml`; a synthetic PR adding a `t("new.key")` without a catalog entry fails it (covered as a fixture test).
- [ ] Non-source locale gaps are reported as counts (warning), errors under `--strict-translations`; unused/orphaned keys remain errors.
- [ ] The 2 conditional-default sites are listed as `uncatalogued` with rationale; runtime-rendered English is unchanged everywhere (no behavior change from cataloging).
- [ ] Checker fixture tests red/green per failure mode; existing script-test lane green.

62:T136a,## Summary

When `composeState` coalesces a provider execution (`providerExecutionsInFlight`, keyed `message.id + provider + audience`), every later caller awaits a promise wired to the **first** caller's cancellation signal and deadline. The waiter cannot cancel the work it is awaiting, and the first caller's abort propagates into turns that never requested it.

I am implementing this (file-and-fix); the PR will be coordinated with #17508's landing since that PR rewrites the same hunks (see coordination note below).

## Reproduced on `develop` (`9bdcf068b`) — and it survives on the #17508 head

`packages/core/src/runtime.ts` (develop):

- `:4590` — `providerSignal` is resolved once per `composeState` call (`turnControllers.signalFor(message.roomId) ?? getStreamingContext()?.abortSignal`).
- `:4617-4626` — the promise is built with **that** signal and **that** caller's budget: `withProviderDeadline((signal) => …, providerBudgetMs, provider.name, providerSignal)`.
- `:4605` / `:4645` — a later caller that hits the map does a raw `const result = await execution.promise;` — no race against the *waiting* caller's own signal, no reference counting.

Consequences (both directions):

- **Waiter B aborts →** nothing is cancelled. B's turn continues consuming A's execution until A's deadline/settlement, so B's "stop" does not stop provider work.
- **Owner A aborts (or times out) →** B's await rejects with A's `TurnAbortedError`/timeout, and B's turn dies with a cancellation its user never issued. The current-signal outcome classification at `:4667` labels it with B's `providerSignal` state, so the trajectory can even *misattribute* whose abort it was.

On the #17508 head (`ebea48b`), the deadline is replaced by owner cancellation but the coalescing shape is identical (`withProviderCancellation(…, providerSignal)` captured once; waiters await verbatim) — I re-derived both directions there in https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/17508#issuecomment-5150514957, and @lalalune independently reproduced the same two items on the prior head (2026-08-01T08:49Z review comment on #17508). So this defect is a property of the coalescing pattern, not of either deadline/cancellation architecture.

## Repair proposal

Keep one shared underlying execution, give each waiter its own boundary:

1. At the await site, race `execution.promise` against the **current** caller's `providerSignal` so a waiter's abort detaches that waiter immediately (rejecting with *its* reason, not the owner's).
2. Reference-count the underlying execution; abort the shared work only when every attached waiter has aborted/detached — a shared execution with one live waiter keeps running.
3. Classify each caller's outcome against its own signal (the `:4667` classification already reads the current signal; it just never gets the chance today because the shared rejection arrives first).

Works identically on the deadline architecture (develop) and the owner-cancellation architecture (#17508).

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A coalesced waiter whose own signal aborts stops awaiting immediately and rejects with its own reason; the shared execution keeps running for the remaining owner.
- [ ] The first caller's abort no longer rejects other waiters with the first caller's reason; each waiter resolves normally if the underlying work settles, or rejects with its own abort.
- [ ] The shared execution is aborted exactly when the last attached caller aborts (reference counting), covered by a deterministic unit test with two concurrent `composeState` calls on the same `message.id + provider`.
- [ ] Existing coalescing behavior (single execution per key, cleanup on settle) unchanged; `bun run --cwd packages/core test` green.

## Evidence plan

- Real-LLM trajectories: N/A — deterministic runtime concurrency behavior; deterministic unit tests drive the real `composeState` path.
- Logs: failing test on current develop (both directions), passing after; full test-lane run.
- Screenshots/video: N/A — no UI.
- Domain artifact: the failing-then-passing test transcript.

## Coordination note

#17508 rewrites these hunks. To avoid churning a core PR: if #17508 lands first, my fix targets its `withProviderCancellation` shape; if this lands first, it is a small, rebASE-friendly change to the await site + map entry. Either way the failing tests are architecture-agnostic (they assert waiter-boundary semantics, not implementation).

## Summary

Hardens the existing Google Calendar push callback path as one security boundary:

- registers no public callback route unless validated runtime webhook settings explicitly enable it
- verifies a destination-bound `gcalwh.v1` HMAC capability before service resolution or DB access
- authorizes account, grant, and audience before any notification, lease, receipt, limiter, or provisional resource-binding write
- applies independent durable principal and destination limits after authentication and authorization
- supports a bounded current/previous key ring plus explicit emergency revocation
- writes a typed immutable ingress receipt transactionally with exactly-once enqueue semantics
- preserves header/body bounds, replay suppression, conditional SQL advancement, leases, retries, and provider refetch

## Security proof

Focused tests traverse the real plugin route/runtime and PGlite production schema:

- default config has no route
- disabled, malformed, bad-signature, stale, wrong-resource, replayed, and mismatched-account callbacks execute no provider sync and create no receipt/pending state
- stateless rejects do not resolve `CalendarService`
- rate-limited authenticated callbacks return `429` before receipt/enqueue/sync
- previous-key callbacks work during rotation and fail after explicit revocation while the key remains configured
- one valid callback processes once and persists one bounded receipt with key/account/grant/calendar provenance

- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-calendar format:check`
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-calendar lint:check`

- `git diff --check origin/develop...HEAD`

# Risks

Low and interaction-only. This changes pager release settlement so a fast intentional flick chooses the adjacent page instead of bouncing back. Direction is limited to the adjacent page, vertical gestures remain rejected, and click suppression is covered in a real Chromium interaction.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It gives fast horizontal flicks a velocity-aware release projection while keeping slow drags distance-based. Release direction is derived from the actual gesture, clamped to one adjacent page, and settled through the existing pager animation path. A style-resolution barrier preserves the final dragged frame before the controlled page target is applied, preventing Chromium from coalescing release and settle into a visible snap.

## What kind of change is this?

UI interaction bug fix.

# Testing

- `bun run --cwd packages/ui test -- src/hooks/useHorizontalPager.test.tsx` — 23/23 passed.
- `node packages/ui/src/components/shell/__e2e__/run-home-screen-e2e.mjs` — real Chromium passed, including final `pointermove` + release in one browser task, immediate `-90px` release preservation, monotonic adjacent-page frame samples, click suppression, and vertical rejection.

66:T1aeb,`hardenIncomingUserMessage` wraps external `content.text` in the untrusted-content security envelope in place. Every action that fell back to `message.content.text` as a query, name, or target was therefore holding the full envelope (markers + provenance header + payload), and several echoed it verbatim to the user — a live leak of the security armor into chat (tj-2dc95f75456876).

Fixes, applied repo-wide (~60 files):

- `unwrapUserMessageText` — a shared read-side seam in core that returns the payload for envelope-wrapped messages and trimmed raw text otherwise; exported from every core entrypoint
- shape-gated display helpers (`describeUserReference` / `userReferenceLogView` and per-plugin equivalents) so blob-shaped references are described, never echoed
- every `message.content.text` fallback across the action surface unwrapped; every user-visible echo clamped
- task `originalRequest` stores the unwrapped payload, not the armor
- honest routing copy for app-create/deploy asks, replacing text that implied the wrong lane
- `outbound-envelope-tripwire`: if the envelope ever reaches an outbound message again through any future path, MESSAGE_SENT reports it via `runtime.reportError` with a clamped preview — detection only, never a silent rewrite

Regression tests added at every fixed site (envelope-echo suites across app-control, cloud-apps, agent-skills, music, calendar, linear, personal-assistant, agent-orchestrator, and core), plus incoming-message-security coverage of the seam itself. All touched-package suites green; typecheck clean across the nine touched packages. Core security/feature suites re-run after the lint fixup: 119 files, 904 tests green.

6a:T3776,Closes #17044

Every authoritative Bun runtime declaration in the repository now resolves the one published canonical version from `.github/ci-bun-version.json` (`1.3.14`), and the CI contract is rewritten so no floating, mutable, implicit, divergent, or unbound declaration can come back.

## What was broken on `develop` (re-verified at `9bdcf068b` before starting)

- Root `package.json` declared `packageManager: bun@1.4.0` — **no such release exists** (npm latest: `1.3.14`), while `.github/ci-bun-version.json` and the root type anchors pinned `1.3.14`.
- `packages/feed/package.json` and `packages/elizaos/templates/project/package.json` declared the unresolvable `bun@1.4.0-canary.1` — every generated project shipped a broken toolchain declaration.
- `.github/actions/setup-bun-workspace` defaulted `bun-version` to `canary` across its **112 call sites**.
- ~30 workflow literals, ~25 `BUN_VERSION` env declarations, and one Windows matrix cell floated on `canary`; 10 `oven-sh/setup-bun@v2` refs were mutable; 2 setup-bun uses wired no version at all (the action's own default is floating `latest`); 4 `bun.sh/install` shell installs on deploy/release hosts installed `canary` or bare latest.
- `build-linux-iso.yml` resolved its Bun **at runtime from root `packageManager`** — it has been asking setup-bun for the unpublished `1.4.0` and dying at setup with the 404 that #17554's coordination comment reports. This was the one place that treated `packageManager` as the source of truth, and it is exactly the place the drift broke first.
- The existing contract guarded only its nine `GATE_WORKFLOWS` and was deliberately silent about all of the above — every one of these coexisted with a green contract run.

## What this PR does

**Commit 1 — unify every declaration (102 files).**
- Root, Feed, and generated-project-template `packageManager` → `bun@1.3.14`.
- Every floating workflow literal, env declaration, and matrix cell → the canonical pin with one uniform comment pointing at the source of truth.
- All `setup-bun@v2` refs → the reviewed commit SHA `0c5077e5…` (verified equal to the upstream `v2` tag object, and already used by the other 75 sites — behaviorally a no-op, immutably so).
- `setup-bun-workspace` default `canary` → the canonical pin (covers all 112 call sites that rely on the default).
- The two implicit `setup-bun` uses (mobile-resource-workbench) gain explicit pins; the four `bun.sh/install` lines pin `bun-v1.3.14` (deploy hosts only install when `bun` is absent, so existing hosts are untouched — no forced downgrade).
- `build-linux-iso.yml`'s runtime resolution step is replaced by the contract-enforced literal: the sync guarantee it hand-rolled (workflow ↔ packageManager) is now provided globally by the contract, without the failure mode it just demonstrated.
- Stale comments corrected everywhere they described the deleted canary tracking, the nonexistent `1.4.0`/`1.4.0-canary.1`, or contradicted the pinned value below them (`view-bundle-size`, `memperf`, `windows-ci` still said "ask for the moving canary channel"). The accurate durable comments (e.g. `cloud-cf-deploy`'s canary link-phase hang note, `release-electrobun`'s version-sensitivity warning) are kept.

**Commit 2 — rewrite the contract so this class of drift fails in repo tests.**
`packages/scripts/ci-bun-version-contract.mjs` now validates, statically, against the whole checked-in tree:
1. every `bun-version`/`BUN_VERSION` value in **workflows and composite actions** — scalar, flow-list, and block-list forms; `${{ env.BUN_VERSION }}` / `${{ matrix.bun-version }}` indirection is allowed only because the same-file declarations it resolves to are themselves validated; anything else is an **unbound expression** and fails;
2. every `oven-sh/setup-bun` ref against a reviewed-SHA set (mutable tags fail), and every use must wire an explicit version (implicit = floating `latest`, fails);
3. every `bun.sh/install` line pins `bun-v<canonical>`;
4. every `packageManager` declaring Bun equals `bun@<canonical>`; root `@types/bun`/`bun-types` anchors must equal the canonical exactly; non-root workspace type declarations are **classified advisory-only** (exact-canonical / compatible-range / drift / unparseable) per the issue's scope — they are compatibility signals, not runtime selectors;
5. a composite action's `bun-version` input default must be canonical;
6. the nine gate lanes must additionally wire the canonical literal directly (unchanged invariant, kept as defense in depth).

Deliberate floating cells (e.g. a future upstream-compat canary matrix) require an explicit file-scoped `FLOATING_ALLOWLIST` entry with a reason — **the allowlist ships empty**, so any float is a reviewable diff, can only run in addition to the pinned lane, and can never become a default or gate a release. Violations aggregate into a single run that names every stale site, which is what makes a future version bump a one-PR mechanical operation.

The full classified scan is emitted as a **machine-readable inventory** (`--inventory`), uploaded as the `bun-runtime-inventory` artifact from `test.yml` on every run — currently 435 sites: `canonical=138, reviewed-sha=85, resolvable-expression=118, exact-canonical=20, compatible-range=72, drift=2`. The two `drift` entries are the pre-existing exact `bun-types: 1.3.13` pins in `configbench`/`personality-bench`, surfaced for maintainer judgment rather than churned here (changing them would touch `bun.lock` for a non-runtime signal).

**Synthetic red tests** (`packages/scripts/__tests__/ci-bun-version-contract.test.ts`, 20 tests / 39 assertions) cover each failure mode individually: divergent concrete pin, floating gate, gate missing its pin, floating source of truth, mutable tag with a canonical version, implicit setup-bun, floating matrix cell in flow AND block form, unbound step-output expression, unpinned `bun.sh/install`, `packageManager` drift, root type-anchor drift, floating composite default — plus green-path tests for the pinned shell install, canonical composite default, allowlist passage, the range classifier, and a real-repo scan asserting zero floating/unreviewed sites remain.

## Out of scope, per the issue

Embedded Android/RISC-V Bun binary artifacts are a separate shipping boundary (`packages/app-core/scripts/bun-riscv64/` is a validated custom Rust-core build with its own device-proof record) — untouched, and deliberately not scanned by the contract.

## Acceptance criteria mapping

- [x] Root packageManager, CI version manifest, Feed packageManager, and generated-project template use one currently published exact Bun version (`1.3.14`).
- [x] Root `@types/bun` and `bun-types` align exactly; the machine-readable inventory classifies every workspace type dependency (compatible range / drift), with the platform-artifact boundary excluded.
- [x] Every authoritative Actions Bun setup resolves the canonical exact version; `canary`/`latest`/implicit/divergent/unbound values fail repo tests.
- [x] Non-authoritative canary requires an explicit file-scoped allowlist entry (shipped empty), additive-only, and cannot gate releases or rewrite the lockfile.
- [x] The contract scans every workflow, composite action, runtime manifest, standalone manifest, and template; synthetic red tests cover mutable/floating/implicit/divergent values and indirection.
- [x] Every setup-bun action use is pinned to a reviewed commit SHA.
- [x] Stale comments corrected.
- [x] Frozen install, contract tests, and workflow lint verified locally (logs below); hosted-platform lanes run on this PR's CI.
- [x] Embedded platform Bun artifacts untouched and explicitly out of scope.

6b:T1db0,Closes #17605

`en.json` is the source catalog every other locale keys off — and it had drifted **705 keys behind source**, because the strict checker built to prevent exactly that (`packages/scripts/check-i18n.mjs`) was invoked from **no CI lane, no package script, nothing**. `defaultValue` masked every miss for English users, which is why it rotted silently; all seven non-English locales render English for those strings and could never translate them. The reverse rot accumulated too: **469 dead keys** (referenced by no literal, no source template prefix, no allowlist entry) sat in every locale, plus one key translated in all seven locales that never existed in en and is used nowhere.

## Commit 1 — catalog repair (locale JSONs only)

- **703 entries restored** to `en.json`, each value extracted **verbatim from its call site's inline `defaultValue`** (zero conflicting defaults across duplicate sites — no invented copy; byte-for-byte what English users already see). Pure line insertions: `703 insertions(+), 0 deletions(-)` on the file's existing formatting.
- **3,741 dead lines purged** across all eight locales (the 469 dead keys everywhere they existed + the seven-locale orphan `mediagalleryview.SearchActive`). Pure line removals; every file re-parsed after edit. Each deletion is checker-derived: uncovered by literals AND source template prefixes AND the allowlist.
- The 2 remaining source-missing keys (`settings.cloudOverview.localModeLabel`/`Description`) pass **runtime-conditional** defaults (ternaries on cloud-connection state). Cataloging them would freeze one branch and silently change rendered English (catalog wins over `defaultValue`), and splitting them is a `packages/ui` surface change requiring visual evidence — deliberately out of scope. They are declared, with reasons, in the new `uncatalogued` allowlist field, and the checker fails if such an entry ever outlives its call site.

## Commit 2 — make the checker wireable, then wire it

The old contract (any locale missing any key = error) made the gate permanently red — presumably why it ended up wired nowhere. New contract, aligned with what CI can hold on every PR:

- **Errors**: literal key missing from the source locale; unused source-locale key; orphaned translation (locale key absent from source); stale `uncatalogued` entry.
- **Warnings** (errors under `--strict-translations`): non-source locales missing used keys / lagging the source catalog — translation debt stays *visible* on every run without blocking unrelated PRs.
- `runI18nCheck` exported pure (no console/exit) with **10 fixture tests** covering each failure mode red and green, following the `ci-bun-version-contract` test pattern; the real-repo test asserts `ok:true` **and** that translation-gap warnings are present (a silent pass would hide the backfill the same way the unwired checker did).
- Wired into `test.yml` beside the other contracts, so no new key can ship uncataloged again.

6c:T125d,## Root cause and production path

Follow-up to closed #17210. Shaw's review was correct: that attempt patched `eliza-sandbox-bridge.ts`, a legacy path not used by realtime voice.

The live path is:

`voice/session/ws` → `internal-eliza-conversation-fetch` → `handleCanonicalScopedAgentStream` → `coordinateSharedStream` → `SharedRuntimeConversation` Durable Object → `SharedRuntimeChatService.stream()`.

On current `develop`, a barge-in aborts the voice session's Eliza SSE fetch. That cancels the DO response body, but `SharedRuntimeChatService.stream()` persists only after the provider's finish part. The spoken user turn and partial assistant response disappear from the shared transcript and next-turn context.

## Fix

- Finalize on consumer cancellation, provider drop, and stream failure.
- Persist the user utterance and partial reply with `interrupted: true`.
- Use stable request-derived message IDs and an idempotent/retryable finalizer, preventing duplicate assistant turns under completion/cancel races.
- Merge history by message ID rather than overwriting a whole capped snapshot.
- Make the DO durable write precede in-memory mutation.
- Serialize Postgres merge with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`.
- Annotate interrupted assistant fragments before they re-enter model context.

No work is added before first token. All added persistence is on completion/interruption paths.

## Bidirectional proof

The exact cancellation canary was run with the test from this PR against unmodified `origin/develop` implementation files:

```text
Expected history length: 3
Received history length: 1
0 pass, 1 fail
```

On this head:

```text
(pass) stream response-body cancellation awaits interrupted history persistence
1 pass, 0 fail
```

Additional focused suites, each run in isolation to avoid Bun cross-file mock pollution:

```text
shared-runtime-chat.test.ts: 14 pass, 0 fail
run-shared-agent-turn.error-policy.test.ts: 8 pass, 0 fail
shared-runtime-conversation.test.ts: 5 pass, 0 fail
shared-rest-adapter.test.ts: 21 pass, 0 fail
```

The tests assert awaited persistence before the DO room lock releases, `interrupted:true`, stable IDs, retry after durable-write failure, no duplicate finalization, and REST transcript visibility.

## Collision check

- #17517 owns UI interaction/voice ownership and does not touch these canonical cloud files.
- #17210 remains closed and unmerged; this PR is its production-path replacement.
- #16711 is merged historically but also patched the legacy bridge, so it does not supply this invariant.
- No workflow, deployment, production, or check changes.

Live first-party staging audio/TTFT remains a post-merge/deploy canary. Current staging health SHA is `2c3d63d3849a36be025387ef39487d959cc2ee02`; production is `d8f47c24997bf8383e616b3eb5397c33cde0609d`. Neither contains this commit.

## Review follow-up

- Fixed the Cloud Tests startup failure from the first head: `SharedRuntimeConversation` now imports the history cap from a side-effect-free policy module, preserving the Worker global-scope lazy-import boundary.
- Exact-head focused rerun: shared runtime chat 14/14 and Durable Object conversation 5/5 passed.
6d:T17b6,Three live failure classes on weak planner models in unaddressed group turns, all reproduced from real trajectories:

- filler completions: the planner produces a contentless acknowledgement in a turn nobody addressed to the agent
- tool-text echo: after an evaluator protocol failure, a replanned weak model repeats planner-facing tool result text verbatim into chat (tj-f730d907139bb2)
- internal error-context bleed: RECENT_ERRORS composed into stage-1 on ambient turns leaks internal failure prose into casual group replies

Structural fixes (no message-text regexes):

- `ambient_turn_policy` planner instruction gated on the structural addressed-turn classifier, never on message text
- deliberate silence is recorded as the terminal the model actually chose (STOP vs IGNORE), with ack-fallback suppression so the delivery floor does not manufacture a filler reply over an intentional silence
- RECENT_ERRORS excluded from stage-1 composition on unaddressed turns; error context stays scoped to turns where the agent was actually addressed
- task-history planner-facing result texts rewritten machine-shaped ("Report the count to the user in your own words") so a protocol-failure echo cannot pass as a chat reply, and the stale in-file comment claiming the deterministic fallback relays `result.text` corrected

Tests: 100+ new/updated across message-runtime-stage1, planner-happy-path, stage1-provider-execution, recent-errors, and task-history suites — all green, typecheck clean on core + agent-orchestrator.

6f:Te23,## Summary

- reject caller-authored `system` messages at the public per-agent A2A boundary
- preserve ordinary `user` and `assistant` conversation history
- fail before credit reservation or provider dispatch

Bearer/session/API-key authentication proves the input principal, not authority to author policy beside the destination agent's operator-owned system prompt. Until agent-signed role provenance exists, accepting `system` here is an unsigned privilege elevation.

This is the smallest enforcement slice from the Soliza federated-charter audit. It does not implement federation, change connector policy, or duplicate trusted-delivery-audience/canonical-memory work.

# Sync with develop

# Risks

Medium. This changes authentication headers on protected loopback self-API calls across agent actions and three plugins. The main risks are canonical/legacy token precedence mistakes or a caller being omitted. Those are covered by shared-helper unit tests, per-fetch AST inventory enforcement, a synthetic bypass red test, and real TCP authentication tests across every named caller. No remote API, transaction, wallet, database schema, or rendered UI behavior changes.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds one shared `createSelfApiRequestHeaders()` resolver for the canonical `ELIZA_API_TOKEN`, legacy `ELIZA_API_AUTH_TOKEN`, alias-aware lookup, and Bearer normalization.
- Routes the protected agent, app-control, Polymarket, and Hyperliquid loopback `/api` callers through that helper instead of ad-hoc or missing auth logic.
- Adds an AST-level contract test that classifies every individual local fetch call, documents narrow non-self-API exemptions, inventories the expected protected paths, and proves a second unauthenticated fetch in the same function is rejected.
- Adds real Node TCP coverage with `ELIZA_REQUIRE_LOCAL_AUTH=1` for canonical, legacy, missing, and incorrect credentials while preserving structured caller failures.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix / security hardening (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is needed. The compatibility contract and exemption rationale are executable in the shared helper tests and repository contract guard.

# Maintainer repair review\n\nThe server now uses the same canonical token precedence and Bearer normalization as every protected caller, and the plugin probe deadline is 60 seconds so the server-side 50-second health deadline has transport headroom. Real \x60startApiServer\x60 coverage proves immediate token rotation, legacy fallback, canonical precedence, concurrent authenticated requests, structured 401 responses, and rejected-delete isolation. 71:T112c,## Problem

The Stage-1 message prompt placed volatile provider output, especially CURRENT_TIME, before the large append-only conversation tail. Cerebras caches exact 128-token prefix blocks, so this ordering invalidated almost the entire reusable prefix every turn. The live Gemma 4 baseline reused only 41.57% of prompt tokens at p50 despite a stable system prompt.

The latency harness also summarized wall/model/provider time without preserving the exact production model input or provider-reported prompt-cache and queue timings. That made a green-looking report possible without proving what the model actually received.

## Fix

- Keep the stable system prompt first, then order the user message as current-turn boundary, chronological prior dialogue, volatile provider output, and the final current message.
- Preserve the semantic current-turn invariant without positional above/below wording.
- Capture the exact RESPONSE_HANDLER input for every warmup, sample, and cancellation invocation, including prompt segments and cache options but only allow-listed fields.
- Report prompt, cached, uncached, and cache-rate token distributions from Cerebras usage; missing cache telemetry is an explicit error rather than a fabricated zero.
- Preserve privacy-safe nested cached-token and provider time_info fields in the cloud probe.
- Give every related direct, gateway, and paired request a stable prompt_cache_key.
- Add observations only. There is no latency or cache threshold, ratchet, timeout gate, or changed-production-code test policy.

Fixes #17509

The report embeds that Git head and `treeClean: true`; the harness now refuses to emit evidence from a dirty tracked tree.

- retain Playwright failure reports and allow the real suite a 30-minute job budget
- refresh the four reviewed Linux baselines affected by the final render boundary and Geist migration

The workflow no longer grants `contents: write`, deletes baselines, commits generated pixels, or pushes from a test job.

## Root cause

The current failure has two layers. `/get-started` baselines predated the homepage font migration, producing deterministic 84%/59% diffs on every retry. Separately, the landing readiness marker was emitted by chat completion alone, before the final R3F camera projection and shader frame were guaranteed to reach the canvas. CI also ran two software-WebGL workers concurrently and could silently regenerate/push missing expectations.

This fixes the rendering boundary and the workflow policy rather than increasing screenshot tolerances or adding sleeps.

## Exact-head verification

Head: `600fcbf918b79221b7e04147ec830199eed53dc7`
Base: `0f8699cebd60cd9bba3b8545ba0739e8e1ceb96f`

- `bun run typecheck` in `packages/homepage`: passed
- Biome on all changed source/workflow files: passed
- `actionlint .github/workflows/quality.yml`: passed
- structural WebGL/browser suite: steady canvases, Telegram replay, rapid reversal passed; interrupted Try transition passed independently after its test budget was aligned with the software-rendered path
- all four changed Linux baselines were opened and manually reviewed
- exact-head checks for the rebased patch are running; the aggregate patch-id is unchanged

Refs #17422

Updates `cryptography` from 48.0.1 to 50.0.0
<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/pyca/cryptography/blob/main/CHANGELOG.rst">cryptography's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<p>50.0.0 - 2026-07-31</p>
<pre><code>
* **SECURITY ISSUE**:
  :func:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.serialization.pkcs7.pkcs7_decrypt_der`
  and its PEM and S/MIME variants no longer expose distinguishable errors or
  timing when unwrapping a ``RecipientInfo``'s ``encryptedKey``, which could
  act as a Bleichenbacher oracle for callers that decrypt untrusted messages.
  A random key is now substituted on failure, as described in :rfc:`3218`.
  Credit to **@X1AOxiang** for reporting the issue
* Deprecated Diffie-Hellman key exchange over finite fields (FFDH).
  Everything FFDH is deprecated, including the types in
  ``cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.dh`` and loading FFDH keys or
  parameters with the key loading APIs. Users should migrate to a more
  modern key exchange algorithm.
* Added ``xof()`` class methods to
  :class:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.hashes.SHAKE128` and
  :class:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.hashes.SHAKE256` for constructing
  algorithm instances configured for use with
  :class:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.hashes.XOFHash`.
* The :mod:`X.509 verification &lt;cryptography.x509.verification&gt;` APIs are now
  considered stable and are subject to our API stability policy.
* Added the :doc:`/cobblestone` recipe, an implementation of the
  Cobblestone-128 and Cobblestone-256 instantiations of the `C2SP
  chunked-encryption specification
  &lt;https://c2sp.org/chunked-encryption&gt;`_ for streaming authenticated
  encryption of large messages.
* Parsing a Signed Certificate Timestamp list now rejects encodings that
  carry trailing bytes after the list or after an individual SCT, instead of
  silently ignoring them.
* Added support for using :class:`~cryptography.x509.Name` as a field type in
  the :doc:`/hazmat/asn1/index` module.
* Loading a public key or an EC private key now rejects DER where the
  ``subjectPublicKey`` (or EC ``publicKey``) ``BIT STRING`` declares a non-zero
  number of unused bits, instead of silently ignoring it.
* Parsing a CRL entry's ``InvalidityDate`` extension now rejects a
  ``GeneralizedTime`` that carries fractional seconds or another non-DER form,
  matching the strict encoding already required for every other X.509 time
  field.
* :func:`~cryptography.x509.ocsp.load_der_ocsp_request` and
  :func:`~cryptography.x509.ocsp.load_der_ocsp_response` now reject a request
  or response whose ``version`` field is not ``v1``, the only version defined
  by RFC 6960, matching the version validation already performed when loading
  certificates, CSRs and CRLs.
* :class:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.hashes.XOFHash` is now supported
  when building against AWS-LC.
* HMAC (and therefore PBKDF2-HMAC) with SHA-3 hashes is now supported when
  building against AWS-LC.
* Diffie-Hellman (:doc:`/hazmat/primitives/asymmetric/dh`) is now supported
  when building against AWS-LC.
&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt; 
</code></pre>
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

Updates `aiohttp` from 3.14.1 to 3.14.3
<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/aio-libs/aiohttp/blob/master/CHANGES.rst">aiohttp's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h1>3.14.3 (2026-07-22)</h1>
<h2>Bug fixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed the client dropping only the first <code>Authorization</code>, <code>Cookie</code> and
<code>Proxy-Authorization</code> header when a redirect crossed an origin -- by :user:<code>arshsmith1</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
:issue:<code>13180</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed error message construction in the C HTTP parser -- by :user:<code>bdraco</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
:issue:<code>13222</code>.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h1>3.14.2 (2026-07-20)</h1>
<h2>Bug fixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed :py:attr:<code>~aiohttp.web.StreamResponse.last_modified</code> rounding a
:class:<code>datetime.datetime</code> with a fractional second down.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
:issue:<code>5303</code>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed resolving <code>localhost</code> on Windows to fall back without <code>AI_ADDRCONFIG</code>
when the first lookup fails, so <code>localhost</code> still works without an active
network.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
:issue:<code>5357</code>.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>

Updates `cryptography` from 49.0.0 to 50.0.0
<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/pyca/cryptography/blob/main/CHANGELOG.rst">cryptography's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<p>50.0.0 - 2026-07-31</p>
<pre><code>
* **SECURITY ISSUE**:
  :func:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.serialization.pkcs7.pkcs7_decrypt_der`
  and its PEM and S/MIME variants no longer expose distinguishable errors or
  timing when unwrapping a ``RecipientInfo``'s ``encryptedKey``, which could
  act as a Bleichenbacher oracle for callers that decrypt untrusted messages.
  A random key is now substituted on failure, as described in :rfc:`3218`.
  Credit to **@X1AOxiang** for reporting the issue
* Deprecated Diffie-Hellman key exchange over finite fields (FFDH).
  Everything FFDH is deprecated, including the types in
  ``cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.dh`` and loading FFDH keys or
  parameters with the key loading APIs. Users should migrate to a more
  modern key exchange algorithm.
* Added ``xof()`` class methods to
  :class:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.hashes.SHAKE128` and
  :class:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.hashes.SHAKE256` for constructing
  algorithm instances configured for use with
  :class:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.hashes.XOFHash`.
* The :mod:`X.509 verification &lt;cryptography.x509.verification&gt;` APIs are now
  considered stable and are subject to our API stability policy.
* Added the :doc:`/cobblestone` recipe, an implementation of the
  Cobblestone-128 and Cobblestone-256 instantiations of the `C2SP
  chunked-encryption specification
  &lt;https://c2sp.org/chunked-encryption&gt;`_ for streaming authenticated
  encryption of large messages.
* Parsing a Signed Certificate Timestamp list now rejects encodings that
  carry trailing bytes after the list or after an individual SCT, instead of
  silently ignoring them.
* Added support for using :class:`~cryptography.x509.Name` as a field type in
  the :doc:`/hazmat/asn1/index` module.
* Loading a public key or an EC private key now rejects DER where the
  ``subjectPublicKey`` (or EC ``publicKey``) ``BIT STRING`` declares a non-zero
  number of unused bits, instead of silently ignoring it.
* Parsing a CRL entry's ``InvalidityDate`` extension now rejects a
  ``GeneralizedTime`` that carries fractional seconds or another non-DER form,
  matching the strict encoding already required for every other X.509 time
  field.
* :func:`~cryptography.x509.ocsp.load_der_ocsp_request` and
  :func:`~cryptography.x509.ocsp.load_der_ocsp_response` now reject a request
  or response whose ``version`` field is not ``v1``, the only version defined
  by RFC 6960, matching the version validation already performed when loading
  certificates, CSRs and CRLs.
* :class:`~cryptography.hazmat.primitives.hashes.XOFHash` is now supported
  when building against AWS-LC.
* HMAC (and therefore PBKDF2-HMAC) with SHA-3 hashes is now supported when
  building against AWS-LC.
* Diffie-Hellman (:doc:`/hazmat/primitives/asymmetric/dh`) is now supported
  when building against AWS-LC.
&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt; 
</code></pre>
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

You can trigger Dependabot actions by commenting on this PR:
- `@dependabot rebase` will rebase this PR

- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> major version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's major version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's major version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> minor version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's minor version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's minor version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name>` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency (unless you unignore this specific dependency or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name>` will remove all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name> <ignore condition>` will remove the ignore condition of the specified dependency and ignore conditions
You can disable automated security fix PRs for this repo from the [Security Alerts page](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/network/alerts).

</details>74:Tf54,Reasoning models burn completion tokens deliberating before emitting the required tool call. With `toolChoice: required` and a 1024-token completion budget, an ambiguous ask exhausts the whole budget on reasoning deltas and the provider terminates the stream with an in-stream server error that is indistinguishable from a transient 5xx — reproducing 100% per-ask. Wire capture shows the entire budget consumed by reasoning with no tool call emitted.

- chat planner completion budget 1024 → 4096, with an `ELIZA_PLANNER_MAX_TOKENS` override for operators who need to tune it; same failure class as the coding lane's earlier budget fix (#10132)
- live stream-start retry widened 3 → 5 attempts so a sustained burst of real provider 500s at stream start still recovers; pinned by a sustained-burst test (five failures, sixth attempt delivers), while the no-retry-after-first-token contract stays intact

Tests: planner-loop suite (111) and stream-start-retry shape suite (11) green; typecheck clean on core + plugin-openai.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Requires an explicit `HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE=true` before selecting the self-hosted fleet.
- Routes missing, empty, false, and noncanonical values to GitHub-hosted runners.
- Converts all 225 existing selectors and adds a repository-wide regression contract.
- Documents why fork pull requests need this fail-safe default.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and CI hardening.

# Documentation changes needed?

The workflow README is updated with the explicit opt-in contract and fork-PR rationale.

76:Tb4e,Fixes #17521.

## What changed

- runs every `Quality (Fork)` lane on `ubuntu-24.04`, removing persistent workspace and runner-service state from untrusted fork validation
- pins the fork lane to the canonical released CI Bun version (`1.3.14`)
- isolates setup-bun's Unix `HOME` and Windows `USERPROFILE` by run, attempt, job, and matrix entry in the shared workspace action, cloud setup action, and direct CLI lane
- disables setup-bun's executable cache for those ephemeral paths, preventing per-run cache-version churn while preserving package and Turbo caches
- separates manual exact-head dispatch concurrency from pull-request metadata runs so skipped PR jobs cannot cancel queued proof
- enforces hosted selection for every current and future fork job, derives the Bun pin from `.github/ci-bun-version.json`, binds isolated homes to parsed setup-bun steps, and rejects same-repository pull requests through mutation tests

## Why

Quality (Fork) run 30688698836 assigned Build and CLI smoke to two registrations on the same Hetzner host. Both were canceled inside actions/checkout while `git clean -ffdx` was running; no setup or repository command executed. GitHub's recorded job completion timestamps precede the checkout logs, and the same registrations have produced prior canceled jobs after successful commands. Hosted runners provide a fresh workspace and process boundary for this security-sensitive fork lane.

Failure evidence:
- Build: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30688698836/job/91339469729
- CLI smoke: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30688698836/job/91339469735

- `bun test packages/scripts/__tests__/ci-turbo-cache-contract.test.ts`: 14 passed
- `node --test packages/scripts/ci-workflow-invariants.test.mjs` against the exact commit tree: 24 passed
- `actionlint .github/workflows/quality-fork.yml`: passed
- `git diff --check origin/develop...HEAD`: passed

<!-- evidence-head:e2d936f6052173da04d5d59419316251ac53e729 -->78:T1883,Closes #17602

`providerExecutionsInFlight` stored one promise per `message.id + provider + audience`, wired to the **first** caller's signal and deadline; a later coalesced caller awaited it verbatim (`runtime.ts:4645`, raw `await execution.promise`). Because the turn registry maps a room to its **newest** controller (`turn-controller.ts` `runWith` overwrites without aborting), the realistic sequence is:

1. Turn A is streaming; its `composeState` owns the execution.
2. Turn B (re-delivery of the same message) starts, coalesces onto A's execution.
3. The user taps **stop** → `abortTurn(room)` aborts **B's** controller.
4. Nothing happens. B neither rejects nor resolves until A's provider settles, then **completes as if never cancelled** — the stop is swallowed. Symmetrically, an abort on A's side rejected B with **A's** `TurnAbortedError`.

## The fix

The execution now owns a dedicated `AbortController`, and every caller — owner included — awaits through `awaitProviderExecution`: a per-waiter race against the caller's **own** signal, reference-counted:

- an aborting waiter rejects immediately with **its** reason;
- the shared work keeps running for the remaining callers;
- the provider is aborted exactly when the last interested caller is gone, so a lone caller's abort still reaches the provider (the existing cancellation test's assertions — `PROVIDER_COMPOSITION_ABORTED` report, `TURN_ABORTED` surface, reason propagation — are unchanged and still green).

No behavior change for uncoalesced executions beyond routing the same signal through the same boundary; deadlines (`withProviderDeadline`) are untouched and still shared per-execution as documented.

## Proof

<img width="1529" height="612" alt="17604-red-green" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/da6d194a-ad80-49a4-96e0-cb844010a6e8" />

Deterministic regression test (`compose-state-provider-execution.test.ts`, "lets a coalesced waiter cancel its own turn without killing the owner"): two overlapping `runWith` turns on one room, one shared SLOW provider, `abortTurn` after coalescing, 250ms race to catch the swallow.

<details><summary>red on the previous code → green with the fix (verbatim)</summary>

```
$ bun run --cwd packages/core test -- compose-state-provider-execution   # test added, code unfixed
 Tests  1 failed | 5 passed (6)
 AssertionError: expected 'swallowed' to be an instance of TurnAbortedError
 (the coalesced waiter neither rejected nor resolved 250ms after abortTurn — the user's stop was swallowed)

$ bun run --cwd packages/core test -- compose-state                      # after fix, all compose-state suites
 Test Files  8 passed (8)
 Tests  32 passed (32)

$ bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck
 Done!

$ bunx biome check packages/core/src/runtime.ts packages/core/src/__tests__/compose-state-provider-execution.test.ts
 Checked 2 files. No fixes applied.
```
</details>

## Relation to #17508

#17508 replaces the deadline architecture with owner cancellation but keeps the same coalescing shape, so this defect survives there (re-derived in https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/17508#issuecomment-5150514957; @lalalune independently reproduced it on the prior head). This fix is deliberately small and boundary-shaped: if #17508 lands first I will rebase this onto its `withProviderCancellation` form — the new test asserts waiter-boundary *semantics*, not implementation, so it transfers as-is.

# Risks

Medium. Config persistence is startup-critical. The fallback is limited to Linux-style `EBUSY` replacement failures, writes a complete sanitized overlay using an exclusive randomized 0600 temp file + file fsync + rename + directory fsync, and throws if that commit also fails. Existing overlays remain the write target for canonical config saves so stale state cannot override a later base write. An explicit `ELIZA_PERSIST_CONFIG_PATH` disables the automatic overlay entirely and remains authoritative.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes two failures exposed while enabling first-party views in a container:

1. `saveElizaConfig()` always replaced `eliza.json` with `rename(2)`. A single-file Docker bind mount cannot be replaced and returns `EBUSY`, so plugin enablement appeared live but disappeared after restart. On exactly that error, the agent now atomically commits the full sanitized config to `<state-dir>/<namespace>.config-overlay.json`; `loadElizaConfig()` merges it last on canonical-config boots. An explicit persistence path bypasses the automatic overlay so stale keys cannot leak across deployment modes. Any fallback failure is surfaced, never ignored.
2. Initial startup applies `ELIZA_AGENT_CHARACTER_JSON` and pins `SANDBOX_ROUTE_AGENT_ID`, but the hot-reload closure rebuilt directly from disk config. A plugin-triggered restart could therefore replace the injected Sol character with default Eliza and a name-derived UUID. Initial boot and hot reload now share `applySandboxIdentityFromEnv`, and reload re-pins the routing id.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix, non-breaking.

# Testing

## Develop-red proof

- Bind-mount regression against the develop implementation throws `EBUSY` at `renameSync(tmp, /cfg/eliza.json)` and creates no durable overlay.
- Develop's hot-reload path calls `loadElizaConfig()` followed directly by `buildCharacterFromConfig(freshConfig)`; it never reapplies `ELIZA_AGENT_CHARACTER_JSON` or `SANDBOX_ROUTE_AGENT_ID`.

## Head-green proof

- `config-bind-mount-persistence.test.ts`: simulates canonical rename `EBUSY`, verifies a 0600 durable overlay, unchanged base mount, restart load, second write, fail-closed dual-write failure, explicit-persist precedence over stale overlays, and include/wallet-key sanitization.
- `sandbox-character.test.ts`: applies the same injected Sol identity to initial and fresh reload configs and verifies the stable route id.
- Focused result: **2 files, 18 tests passed**.
- Biome on all five changed files: clean.
- `git diff --check`: clean.
- Package typecheck reaches only unrelated missing optional workspace declarations in the isolated worktree; the prior exact-head CI typecheck passed and the new exact-head CI run is authoritative.

7c:T13f8,## Summary

Discord can emit `messageCreate` immediately after gateway `ClientReady` while async `onReady` is still hydrating Discord application-owner aliases. The account facade also snapshotted `clientReadyPromise` while it was still `null`, because `MessageManager` is constructed before `initializeAccount()` assigns the promise.

An owner message in that window was persisted under the platform-derived entity instead of the canonical owner. Owner-private recall then correctly failed closed for the wrong identity, leaving split identity in canonical memory.

This PR:

- exposes the account ready promise through a live facade getter;
- gates gateway ingress, including listen-only ingestion, until readiness;
- awaits readiness again in MessageManager before Discord dedupe, entity/room persistence, audience attestation, model context, or delivery;
- bounds the readiness wait at 30 seconds and fails closed with `discord:message-before-ready` when ready-time initialization rejects or stalls;
- adds real AgentRuntime + plugin-sql/PGlite production-path proof through `MessageManager`, canonical owner/room participants, destination-bound recall authorization, and exactly one Discord DM send.

## Block-0 and overlap

Checked open PRs and elizaOS Project 12 before code. No open item owns this race.

- #17212 is merged and owns destination-bound private-context policy. This PR uses it unchanged. Owner-in-group stays DENY.
- #17213 is merged and owns optional group coordination. This PR does not change it or its default-off posture.
- #17167 is closed; its outbound DM participant-before-dedupe fix is already on `develop`. This PR does not duplicate outbound delivery scope.
- Canonical-memory and deployment lanes retain their schema/deploy ownership. This PR only closes pre-ingress identity ordering.

## Production path

`service.ts initializeAccount/attemptDiscordLogin` -> `discord-interactions.ts onReady/refreshOwnerDiscordUserIds` -> `discord-events.ts messageCreate/ready gate` -> `messages.ts handleMessage/defense gate` -> `discord-history.ts buildMemoryFromMessage` -> core `ensureConnection` -> canonical delivery-audience attestation -> core MessageService persistence/composition -> Discord callback/send/persist.

## Bidirectional proof

`connector-loop.harness.test.ts` boots a real PGlite-backed `AgentRuntime` with deterministic model proxy and drives the production connector entrypoint.

With the production fix reverted, it fails because one DM is sent before owner hydration (`expected 0, received 1`). With this head it proves:

- no room, memory, or send before readiness;
- one external DM after readiness;
- persisted actor is the canonical owner;
- room participants are exactly owner + runtime agent;
- stored Discord text is recallable;
- owner-private authorization succeeds in DM;
- adding a third participant makes authorization fail closed.

A separate facade regression fails on unmodified develop because it receives the stale snapshotted promise, and passes on head.

7e:T12a7,# Relates to

Closes #17588.

# Risk

- [x] This PR targets `develop` and is rebased onto `origin/develop@9bdcf068bd88d39c82927a861df810d0c7810da1` with zero conflicts.
- [x] A reviewer can confirm the parser behavior and its failure mode from the executable tests and mutation record below.

# Background

`check-pr-evidence.mjs` accepts pasted transcripts for non-visual evidence rows, but its help advertised only links and `N/A`. More importantly, `boundRowBlock` stopped at a blank line inside a fenced transcript. The most natural Markdown form therefore looked valid to an author while the gate silently saw only the checkbox line.

# Implementation

- The failure help now documents all supported evidence forms and the actual `3` line / `120` character transcript floor.
- Transcript measurement and shortfall diagnostics share one parsing path, so help cannot drift from acceptance.
- Evidence rows now preserve complete backtick and tilde fences across a blank line before the fence and blank lines inside it.
- Fence closing follows Markdown semantics: the delimiter character must match and the closer must be at least as long as the opener.
- Unterminated fences fail closed and are truncated before the opener, preventing unrelated headings or links below a row from becoming accidental evidence.
- Regression cases cover complete backtick fences, tilde fences, blank lines, threshold diagnostics, and unterminated-fence bleed.

This is the implementation-level repair. The earlier draft merely documented the fenced-block limitation; that would have left a standard Markdown transcript broken.

# Verification

Exact repaired head: `cbbe424a217a489ed50a3af431efe586d31fab47`
<!-- evidence-head:cbbe424a217a489ed50a3af431efe586d31fab47 -->

- `node scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs --self-test`: 14/14 cases passed.
- `node --test scripts/check-pr-evidence.test.mjs`: 89/89 tests passed.
- scoped Biome and `git diff --check`: passed.
- mutation proof: removing fenced-block blank-line preservation makes `keeps complete fenced transcripts intact across blank lines` fail because only the checkbox remains; restoring it returns the case and full suite to green.

7f:T197a,Closes #17610

**Following the repository's own two contribution documents always produced an invalid attribution marker.** The published ledger shows the scale: **3 valid against 64 missing-or-invalid of 67 eligible sources (95.5% failure)**, including PRs authored by @lalalune (#17424, #17555).

This changes **no scoring** — `leaderboard.ts:1379` states that complete, partial, missing, and invalid attribution states *"add no points."* What was broken is the **provenance dataset the attribution system exists to produce**: `attributions`, `attributionCoverage`, and `invalidAttributionMarkers` currently describe almost nothing that actually happened.

## Root cause

`markerFooterError` required **exactly one** of each visible row across the *entire body*. But `.github/pull_request_template.md:28-37` already ships three of those labels as checklist rows, and `SKILL.md` says *"complete every stable contribution-attribution row in the repository template **and append this footer after the template**"* — where the footer repeats them. (The skill-revision regex accepts both `Skill revision` and `Contribution skill revision`, so the template row and the footer row both count.) Every label appears twice, `length !== 1` fires, marker rejected — contributor did everything right.

## The fix

Count footer rows only within the **terminal attribution block**: the contiguous run of label lines ending at the already-validated lane signature. A checklist row ninety lines above the footer is not a competing footer. Duplicate detection is deliberately unweakened — rows abutting the lane are inside that block and still collide, and the at-most-one-marker gate is untouched.

## Proof

<details><summary>isolating the cause to a single line (real validator)</summary>

```
A. footer alone (no template rows):            valid=1 invalid=0
B. ONE template attribution row above footer:  valid=1 invalid=1  marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
C. the real template's 3 rows above footer:    valid=1 invalid=1  marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
```
</details>

<details><summary>end-to-end: real template + skill footer + the real evidence tool (before → after)</summary>

```
BEFORE
1. .github/pull_request_template.md + footer appended after it:  valid=1 invalid=1  marker requires exactly one complete visible attribution footer
2. after patchRow on a template-present row:                     valid=1 invalid=1  (same)
3. after patchEvidenceHead + a row absent from the body:         valid=1 invalid=1  (same)

AFTER
1. .github/pull_request_template.md + footer appended after it:  valid=1 invalid=0
2. after patchRow on a template-present row:                     valid=1 invalid=0
3. after patchEvidenceHead + a row absent from the body:         valid=1 invalid=0
```
</details>

<details><summary>suite</summary>

```
$ bun test packages/eliza-computer/src/lib/leaderboard.test.ts
 54 pass
 0 fail
```
(52 pre-existing, unchanged, plus two new: the real template's rows above an appended footer validate; adjacent duplicated rows still fail with the original reason.)
</details>

## A second, related defect — reported, not fixed here

`scripts/pr-evidence.mjs` `patchRow:332-339` and `patchEvidenceHead:353-364` **append** when a marker is absent, which relocates content after a trailing attribution footer and trips the separate `"marker must be the final source content"` rule. I hit this on four of my own PRs and repaired them by hand; no instruction tells a contributor to re-order. It is left out of this PR to keep the diff to one concern — happy to send it as a follow-up making those functions insert *before* a trailing footer, if maintainers want it separately.

# Risks

Low. Retries are limited to three full GET attempts with 250 ms and 500 ms waits. The classifier is deliberately narrow: authentication and other non-transient failures still surface immediately, and malformed successful output is never retried or accepted.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Makes the contribution skill's GitHub inventory reads resilient to transient transport failures and GitHub HTTP 5xx responses. Each retry starts the paginated GET from the beginning, so partial output from a failed attempt cannot leak into the final report. Exhausted failures report the attempt count.

The regression suite covers TLS timeout recovery, HTTP 503 recovery, exhaustion, GET-only request semantics, ignored partial output, immediate authentication failure, spawn failure, and malformed successful JSON.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No. This preserves the documented CLI contract and changes only failure handling plus its regression coverage.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Run the focused contribution-skill test, then run the live report command shown in the evidence row. The live command must finish with a complete parsed inventory rather than a partial result.

84:T29c1,Closes #17597.

Adds a unit test for `sliceToFitBudget`, which had none.

## Why this function

It is the only thing bounding the `actionState` provider's action history before
that history reaches the model
([`actionState.ts:203`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/develop/packages/core/src/features/basic-capabilities/providers/actionState.ts#L203),
its sole caller, on the `fromEnd: true` path). A regression here throws nothing —
it either drops the agent's most recent run from context or lets the prompt slip
past `ACTION_HISTORY_TARGET_CHARS`. Both fail quietly.

## What is covered

16 cases across both directions: exact fit (the `>` vs `>=` boundary), first item
too large, the contiguous-slice guarantee, empty input, zero/negative budget,
zero-cost items, single-estimation, and input immutability.

## Proof the suite is load-bearing

A test file that merely restates the implementation passes forever and catches
nothing, so I mutated the real source and confirmed the suite fails. Each row
below is a separate run against a deliberately broken `slice-to-fit-budget.ts`:

| Mutation applied to the source | Suite result | First case to catch it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `> targetChars` → `>= targetChars` (exact-fit off-by-one) | 3 failed / 13 passed | `includes an item that exactly exhausts the budget` |
| `break` → `continue` (drops the contiguity guarantee) | 4 failed / 12 passed | `stops at the first item that does not fit rather than skipping it` |
| `targetChars <= 0` → `targetChars < 0` (guard weakened) | 1 failed / 15 passed | `treats a zero budget as no room even for a zero-cost item` |
| `break` → `continue` in the **`fromEnd`** loop | 2 failed / 14 passed | `returns a contiguous suffix, never a gap` (after @lalalune's fix — see below) |
| `if (items.length === 0) return [];` removed | 16 passed — **survived** | none, and correctly so |

The fourth is reported rather than hidden: that guard is a fast path, not a
behavior. With it gone the forward loop still yields `slice(0, 0)` and the
`fromEnd` loop still yields `slice(0)`, so the function is observably identical.
It is an equivalent mutant, and writing a test that pretended to kill it would be
the exact larp this suite exists to avoid.

The third mutation survived my first draft. Rather than leave the hole I added
the zero-cost-item case, which is what now closes it — the table above is the
re-run, not the original.

Source was restored and verified clean (`git diff --quiet`) after every mutation;
only the test file is added in this PR.

## Checks

`bun run --cwd packages/core test -- src/utils/slice-to-fit-budget.test.ts` →
16 passed. `bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck` → clean.
`bunx biome check` → clean.

85:Tf7c,## Summary

Removes five generated agent-surface browser outputs from source control and routes future repository-wide evidence recordings to the canonical `e2e-recordings/ui-agent-surface/test-results` destination. Direct runner invocations retain their fixture-local output directory.

This repairs the deeper recorder/runner contract rather than only ignoring generated files: the canonical orchestrator now passes an explicit destination, the runner validates all five outputs, and focused contract coverage locks both modes. No production runtime or rendered product behavior changes.

## Changes

- Delete the five checked-in generated HTML/PNG outputs and ignore only their exact fixture-local directory.
- Pass `E2E_RECORDING_DIR` from the canonical recorder to custom and package runners.
- Resolve the agent-surface destination explicitly while preserving direct-run compatibility.
- Fail the Chromium runner when any expected artifact is missing or empty.
- Document the recording contract in the mirrored package guidance.

<!-- evidence-head:44d6d49f976b7a4d7614eec2b20bd55b92a4bdee -->86:T156a,## Summary

Fixes the homepage failures behind develop Quality Extended run [30626491746](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30626491746).

- preserve the logo aspect ratio when consumers set a fixed height
- expose a completion signal for the deferred phone/camera animation
- wait for that signal before visual assertions
- require all 12 route/viewport snapshots, not 10
- regenerate the four Linux baselines changed by #17411
- add a browser regression test for the logo sizing invariant

This is the focused workflow repair. It references, but does not close, #17422; the remaining cross-platform fidelity and verifiable-performance requirements stay tracked there.

## Root cause

The failure reproduced across the failing SHA, its parent, and three earlier develop commits. #17411 added intrinsic logo dimensions without automatic width, changed shader/model output without final Linux baselines, and left the deferred phone animation without a readiness contract. The screenshot harness raced that state.

## Exact-head evidence

Evidence is bound to PR head `d561d2a30c38b981b7454348fbfa7f15c287ca42` and its parent `2711be746311f4e4e883b7afa38be2f11b6959a4`. It is **not** evidence against current `develop`; `develop` advanced after this pair. Fresh production builds of both SHAs were captured with Playwright/Chromium 1.61.1 in the official Noble Linux image at 1280x720 and 390x844.

- full exact-head Linux homepage visual matrix: 12/12 passed
- focused brand contracts plus four affected visuals: 6/6 passed
- console/page errors: 0
- failed network requests and HTTP 4xx/5xx: 0
- screenshots, walkthrough, logs, geometry, and OCR were manually reviewed

Develop run [30649082646](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30649082646) at `770eb9c3fc424dfa2093a11ae365cd9c12014acf` failed `get-started-desktop`, `get-started-mobile`, and `leaderboard-mobile` after retries. All three are directly covered by this PR's changed Linux baselines; `leaderboard-desktop` is the fourth changed baseline. This is coverage corroboration only, not current-develop proof.

## Readiness defect

**Keep this PR in draft.** Manual exact-head review found that `data-phone-model="settled"` fences camera completion but not completion of the chat canvas used by the leaderboard screenshot. At the declared boundary, desktop is missing the final response and mobile is missing the final two messages. The test passes because Playwright's screenshot assertion retries until a later frame. The [marker-frame comparison](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-2/17448-d561d2a-settled-marker-leaderboard-desktop-mobile.jpg) and [walkthrough](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-2/17448-d561d2a-walkthrough.mp4) show the defect directly.

Refs #17422

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased without conflict onto `origin/develop@a38ea13ca8af98314b8ea3b494b3666a352f248e`.
- [x] Exact head: `6b3276b19525b4057701dce3cf8ceca6b42eaa33`.

# Background

`NON_REAL_EVIDENCE_RE` used `\b` boundaries. JavaScript treats punctuation in commands, paths, filenames, and member names as word boundaries, so a real transcript that merely named one of the gate's marker words could void the entire evidence row.

The boundary now requires a marker to stand apart from identifier punctuation on both sides. A dot is treated as identifier punctuation only when it starts an extension or member; sentence-ending punctuation still leaves a bare marker detectable:

```js
/(?<![\w/\\@:.-])(?:placeholder|…|mocks?|fixtures?|…)(?![\w/\\@-]|:[\w-]|\.[\w-])/i
```

This covers hyphenated commands, POSIX and Windows paths, colon-delimited script names, filenames, and object members. Explicit disclosure that evidence is fake, mocked, a placeholder, or otherwise non-real remains rejected.

## Why this is the implementation fix

The original patch protected only `/` and `-`. Maintainer review extended the rule and tests to cover `\\`, `.`, `:`, and `@`, while preserving sentence-ending punctuation. The full test suite now exercises the production evaluator, not only the embedded self-test.

# Testing

The load-bearing control is unchanged: reverting the boundary makes the marker-bearing identifier case fail. On the final rebased head:

```text
$ bun test scripts/check-pr-evidence.test.mjs
93 pass
0 fail
check-pr-evidence self-test passed (14 cases).

$ bunx biome check scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs scripts/check-pr-evidence.test.mjs
Checked 2 files. No fixes applied.

$ git diff --check origin/develop...HEAD
(exit 0)
```

The 93 cases include the merged complete-fence parser and fork-file verification suites, plus the new command/path/member boundary case.
